5:T1350,## Problem

#16207 intentionally changed `NotificationService.start()` to fail when persisted notification state cannot be read instead of treating an unreadable durable inbox as a healthy empty inbox (the acceptance criterion in #16206).

That fail-closed contract is currently lost at the next boundary:

- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.ts` returns HTTP 200 with `{ notifications: [], unreadCount: 0 }` for `GET /api/notifications` whenever `runtime.getService(ServiceType.NOTIFICATION)` is absent.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.ts` has the same fallback for the Android UDS route.
- Neither route distinguishes an intentionally disabled/unregistered service from `pending`, `registering`, or `failed` service state. After a cache-read failure, `AgentRuntime._runServiceStart()` marks the service `failed` and leaves no instance, so both routes convert the failure back into a healthy empty inbox.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.ts` hydrates once. On a transport/5xx failure it logs, sets `hydrated: true`, preserves the live WebSocket subscription, but schedules no HTTP hydration retry. A transient startup race or recovered persistence layer can therefore leave persisted history absent for the whole UI session.
- The HTTP route comment says the UI “degrades gracefully and retries,” but no such retry currently exists.

This is a fail-closed integration/recovery gap. It is **not** the packaged App Live native-notification failure: that test injects a mock WebSocket `agent_event`, does not start core `NotificationService`, and the observed failing run predates #16207.

## Required behavior

1. Preserve #16207's fail-closed persistence semantics. Do not restore the blanket `hydrate()` catch that turns an unreadable inbox into empty state.
2. Give notification routes enough runtime lifecycle information to distinguish:
   - intentionally disabled/unregistered notification support;
   - `pending` / `registering` startup;
   - `failed` startup;
   - a registered service instance.
3. For `pending` / `registering`, return a typed retryable 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_NOT_READY`) and a bounded `Retry-After` value instead of HTTP 200 empty.
4. For `failed`, return a typed 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_FAILED`) without leaking adapter/secrets/internal error details. Never represent failed persistence as an empty successful inbox.
5. Define the intentional disabled/headless contract explicitly. If that case remains HTTP 200 empty, it must be positively identified rather than inferred from a missing service instance.
6. Add bounded, jittered/background UI hydrate retry that:
   - honors `Retry-After` when present;
   - does not clear already received notifications;
   - keeps the live WebSocket subscription active;
   - avoids duplicate subscriptions/delivery;
   - stops after a documented cap and exposes/logs the terminal hydrate error rather than silently reading as healthy empty.
7. Add explicit, single-flight service recovery/backoff for transient notification hydration failures. Recovery may live at the runtime service boundary or in a dedicated route/service coordinator, but requests must not create a retry stampede. A recovered cache adapter must be able to rehydrate persisted history without restarting the entire agent.
8. Apply equivalent semantics to the Android UDS notification route.
9. Emit enough structured diagnostics/telemetry to distinguish disabled, not-ready, failed, retrying, recovered, and terminal-retry-exhausted states.

## Tests

- `packages/core/src/services/notification.test.ts`
  - unreadable cache still fails startup;
  - a transient cache-read failure can recover through the chosen explicit recovery path;
  - concurrent recovery requests are single-flight / bounded.
- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.test.ts`
  - pending/registering GET is typed 503 + `Retry-After`, not 200 empty;
  - failed GET is typed 503, not 200 empty;
  - intentionally disabled/unregistered behavior is explicit;
  - registered service returns persisted rows.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.test.ts` (or the canonical store test file)
  - bounded retry and `Retry-After` handling with fake timers;
  - state received over WS is preserved while HTTP hydrate retries;
  - successful recovery merges/sets persisted history without duplicate delivery;
  - terminal exhaustion is observable and does not create an infinite timer/subscription loop.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.test.ts`
  - lifecycle-state response parity with the HTTP route;
  - recovered service serves persisted rows.

## Non-goals

- Do not conflate this with the separate App Live packaged native-notification test failure.
- Do not mask cache/adapter failures as empty state.
- Do not retry every incoming request independently.

Related: #16206, #16207.
6:T7b9,## Problem

`.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml` accepts `expected_gateway_sha` and verifies that `/api/health` serves that SHA before and after the live paired benchmark. It does not also assert that the workflow checkout's `GITHUB_SHA` equals `expected_gateway_sha`.

If `develop` advances between an exact staging deployment and workflow dispatch, the artifact can combine:

- scripts/config from a newer checkout; and
- a gateway deployment at the requested older SHA.

The served-SHA checks still pass, but the artifact is not intrinsically tied to one source revision. We can close the race externally today, but the workflow should make the invariant self-enforcing.

## Evidence

Read-only audit of the post-merge validation path for #16156 found:

- the canonical deploy guard correctly restricts staging to `develop`;
- Cloud CF Deploy stamps and verifies its exact served SHA;
- Chat Latency Live verifies `expected_gateway_sha` before and after the benchmark;
- no job step compares `github.sha` / `GITHUB_SHA` with `inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.

Historical run 29171752918 is still valid because the run/deploy provenance was checked separately. This issue hardens future artifacts.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Fail before any paid request unless `github.sha == inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.
- [ ] Keep the existing 40-character SHA validation and before/after served-health checks.
- [ ] Emit both checkout and served SHAs in the privacy-safe summary/artifact.
- [ ] Add a self-test or static workflow contract proving a mismatch fails before the benchmark command.
- [ ] Preserve canonical-ref guards; do not add a feature-branch deploy bypass.
- [ ] Document the exact merged-`develop` dispatch sequence.

## Safe interim procedure

Before dispatch, require `refs/heads/develop` to equal the served deployment SHA; immediately after dispatch, require the run's `headSha` to equal the same SHA. Stop without running the benchmark if either check fails.

This is independent of the same run’s Cloud bearer propagation failure and independent of chat latency: the five live local-agent chat turns passed before this batch began.c:Te0a,## Problem

Cloud's shared Anthropic-thinking helper treats all supported Opus 4.x models as manual extended-thinking models and always emits `thinking: { type: "enabled", budgetTokens }`.

On current `develop` (`7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c`):

- [`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/anthropic-thinking.ts:24-42`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/anthropic-thinking.ts#L24-L42) classifies every `claude-opus-4*` model under one capability.
- [`anthropic-thinking.ts:172-197`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/anthropic-thinking.ts#L172-L197) always builds manual `enabled + budgetTokens` options.
- [`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/models/model-tiers.ts:73-76`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/models/model-tiers.ts#L73-L76) makes `anthropic/claude-opus-4.7` the default Ultra model, so this is a live default-model path whenever a CoT budget is configured.

Anthropic documents that Claude Opus 4.7 and 4.8 require adaptive thinking. Manual `thinking: {type:"enabled", budget_tokens:N}` is unsupported and returns HTTP 400; `effort` is the supported control for thinking depth:

- [Anthropic: Effort — behavior by model](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort#effort-with-extended-thinking)
- [Anthropic: Adaptive thinking](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/adaptive-thinking)

The repository's locked `@ai-sdk/anthropic` 3.x provider already exposes adaptive thinking and effort options, so this should be a capability/routing fix rather than a dependency blocker.

## Related work

- #16081 and #16099 govern hard output-cap/admission behavior. Adaptive `effort` is behavioral, not a hard token ceiling, so this fix must coordinate with that contract rather than treating effort as a numeric budget.
- #11525/#11526 concern plugin-level temperature/output overrides; they do not change this cloud helper.

## Scope

- Introduce an explicit Anthropic thinking-capability resolver (`manual`, `adaptive`, or unsupported) instead of a single broad Opus/Sonnet substring rule.
- Emit `thinking: {type:"adaptive"}` for adaptive-only models and never send `budgetTokens` to Opus 4.7/4.8.
- Define and document how existing numeric `ANTHROPIC_COT_BUDGET` / `anthropicThinkingBudgetTokens` settings interact with adaptive-only models. Do not silently describe `effort` as a hard token cap.
- Preserve manual `enabled + budgetTokens` behavior for models that still support it.
- Update environment/config documentation and all cloud call sites consuming the shared helper.

## Acceptance

- [ ] Bare, provider-prefixed, dot, and hyphen aliases for Opus 4.7 and 4.8 resolve to adaptive thinking.
- [ ] Their serialized provider options contain `thinking.type = "adaptive"` and no `budgetTokens`/`budget_tokens`.
- [ ] Supported manual-thinking models retain `enabled + budgetTokens` behavior.
- [ ] Unsupported models emit no thinking options.
- [ ] Any new effort configuration is validated and documented; invalid values fail clearly.
- [ ] Overall `maxOutputTokens` remains the hard admission/provider ceiling and is tested independently from adaptive effort.
- [ ] Shared-helper tests and route-level tests cover chat completions, messages, live A2A, and live MCP consumers.
- [ ] A staging request against Opus 4.7 or 4.8 with thinking enabled succeeds without the manual-thinking 400.

d:T8fc,## Problem

The live per-agent MCP `chat` tool uses different formulas for the output ceiling admitted by billing and the ceiling sent upstream.

On current `develop` (`7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c`):

- [`packages/cloud/api/agents/[id]/mcp/route.ts:317-326`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/mcp/route.ts#L317-L326) estimates/reserves `4096 + effectiveThinkingBudget` (or 4096 without thinking).
- [`route.ts:354-368`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/mcp/route.ts#L354-L368) sends `max(4096, effectiveThinkingBudget) + 4096`, and sends no cap when thinking is disabled.

For a 1024-token thinking budget, billing admits 5120 output tokens while the provider is authorized for 8192. With no thinking, billing assumes 4096 while provider enforcement is implicit/absent.

## Related work

- #16081 requires reservation admission to equal the provider-facing budget.
- #16099 currently covers `/v1/chat/completions`, not the MCP agent route.

## Scope

- Derive one immutable admitted/provider-facing MCP output cap.
- Use it for both `estimateRequestCost` and `streamText.maxOutputTokens`.
- Pass the already-resolved effective thinking policy through provider options rather than recomputing a potentially different value.
- Preserve creator markup, reserve-before-forward ordering, and reconciliation behavior.

## Acceptance

The reservation estimate and provider cap must be identical for at least:

| Effective thinking budget | Reserved/provider cap |
| ---: | ---: |
| none | 4096 |
| 1024 | 5120 |
| 4096 | 8192 |
| 8000 | 12096 |

- [ ] Every successful MCP chat passes the same numeric cap to pricing and `streamText`.
- [ ] Character override, environment default, and environment-cap cases are covered.
- [ ] Insufficient-credit admission performs no provider call.
- [ ] Creator earnings remain gated on successful final collection.

## Regression tests

Extend `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/agent-mcp-billing.test.ts` so thinking resolution is parameterized instead of always mocked to `null`. For each table row, assert the exact final argument to `estimateRequestCost` equals `streamText`'s `maxOutputTokens`.

e:T9ca,## Problem

The live per-agent A2A chat route computes an admitted output ceiling for reservation pricing, but does not pass that ceiling to the provider call.

On current `develop` (`7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c`):

- [`packages/cloud/api/agents/[id]/a2a/route.ts:277-293`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/a2a/route.ts#L277-L293) resolves the Anthropic thinking budget, derives `maxOutputTokens = effectiveThinkingBudget + 500`, and prices the reservation with it.
- [`route.ts:323-332`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/a2a/route.ts#L323-L332) calls `streamText` without `maxOutputTokens`.

The provider can therefore consume more output than the amount admitted by the upfront reservation. Final reconciliation may report an uncollected overage only after upstream inference has already been purchased.

This is the live `agents/[id]/a2a` route, not the exported-but-unwired shared dispatcher tracked historically in #12979.

## Related work

- #16081 establishes the broader invariant that billing admission must use the exact provider-facing budget.
- #16099 currently changes only `/v1/chat/completions`; it does not cover this A2A route.

## Scope

- Derive one admitted/provider-facing output cap for A2A chat.
- Pass the identical value to `estimateRequestCost` and `streamText.maxOutputTokens`.
- Preserve character settings, environment defaults, and `ANTHROPIC_COT_BUDGET_MAX` clamping.
- Keep reserve-before-provider ordering and fail before provider dispatch when credits are insufficient.

## Acceptance

- [ ] The exact value used to price/reserve A2A chat is also sent as `streamText.maxOutputTokens`.
- [ ] Character override, environment default, and environment-capped budget cases are covered.
- [ ] Insufficient-credit admission performs no provider call.
- [ ] Final usage cannot exceed the admitted ceiling because the provider option was omitted.
- [ ] The existing creator-markup and exactly-once reconciliation behavior remains intact.

## Regression tests

Extend `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/agent-a2a-billing.test.ts` with a non-null thinking budget and assert both:

1. `estimateRequestCost(..., admittedMaxOutputTokens)`; and
2. `streamText({ ..., maxOutputTokens: admittedMaxOutputTokens })`.

The test must drive the mounted live route, not a copied helper or the unwired shared A2A dispatcher.

f:T6ea,## Summary

`lookup.ts` now resolves independent input/output pricing reads concurrently to remove serial billing-path latency (#16156). On a fresh isolate where both sides miss the completed-value cache, both tasks can reach the same provider catalog fetch before either result is cached.

This is not a billing correctness bug: invalid/missing prices still fail closed and the returned rates are validated. It is a low-priority duplicate-I/O/cold-latency opportunity.

## Observed shape

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/lookup.ts` starts the input and output lookups concurrently.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/cache.ts` caches completed values, but does not retain an in-flight promise.
- Two same-key cold misses can therefore issue two identical external catalog reads.

## Suggested implementation

Add key-scoped single-flight caching for in-progress catalog resolutions:

- share one promise for concurrent requests for the same canonical provider/model/rate key;
- remove the promise on both fulfillment and rejection;
- keep the existing completed-value TTL cache and fail-closed validation;
- do not let a failed promise poison later retries;
- bound the in-flight map or prove its lifecycle is bounded by completion.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A deterministic test gates the provider/catalog fetch and proves two concurrent same-key cold lookups make exactly one upstream call.
- [ ] Different keys still resolve concurrently.
- [ ] A rejected in-flight fetch is observed by all waiters and a later call retries normally.
- [ ] Invalid/non-finite rates remain rejected; no fallback can under-bill.
- [ ] Typecheck, focused pricing tests, error-policy ratchet, and changed-file coverage pass.

Related: #16156.
10:Tcf6,**Describe the bug**

A clean agent boot can advertise a settled/healthy deferred boot while startup work has failed. Three related paths are currently observable in a fresh packaged-runtime smoke:

- `AgentRuntime` service startup catches exceptions and returns `null` after logging, so the failure never reaches `runtime.reportError` or structured boot health.
- `@elizaos/plugin-inbox` runs its `inbox_migration` service against a fresh PGlite database before the required schema is ready and fails on `ALTER TABLE`.
- `@elizaos/plugin-scheduling` seeds boot tasks before its scheduled-task runner service is available.

This makes a broken initialization pipeline look healthy and violates the fail-fast/observable-boundary error policy.

**To Reproduce**

1. Build and start the real agent with `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`, configuration, HOME, and credential paths pointed at new temporary directories.
2. Load the default first-party plugin set including inbox and scheduling.
3. Wait for the deferred-boot health state to settle.
4. Inspect structured backend logs and the health response.

The logs report service/migration/seed failures while the health surface reports a settled boot.

**Expected behavior**

- A service startup exception is reported through `runtime.reportError` with service/plugin context and is reflected in structured boot failure state.
- Fresh PGlite initialization orders inbox schema work after its prerequisite schema exists; it completes without a suppressed `ALTER TABLE` failure.
- Scheduling boot seeding runs only after the scheduled-task runner service is registered and ready.
- A real isolated fresh-state boot completes without these errors, and injected service failures remain observably failed.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

- N/A - this is a headless agent boot/runtime correctness issue with no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no rendered UI surface.
- Backend logs: package smoke logs show the three failures; exact isolated before/after logs will be attached to the PR after manual review.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A - no frontend path changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or planner behavior changes.
- Domain artifacts: fresh PGlite schema/service registry state and the boot health payload will be captured in the PR evidence.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Reproduce in an isolated state/config/home without ambient credentials.
- [ ] Remove catch-and-null service startup behavior; typed failures are reported and visible at the runtime boundary.
- [ ] Make inbox fresh-schema initialization deterministic and successful on real PGlite.
- [ ] Make scheduling seed ordering depend on runner readiness structurally, not timing.
- [ ] Add real integration coverage for success and injected-failure paths.
- [ ] Run focused tests, error-policy ratchet, and full `bun run verify` after rebasing onto current `origin/develop`.

**Additional context**

This was exposed while hardening real Snap/Flatpak/Debian install-and-boot smoke coverage. The package verifier must not be asked to parse logs to compensate for a runtime health surface that claims success after startup failures.
11:T939,## Problem

`zai-glm-4.7` — the default `TEXT_LARGE` model on Cerebras since #16261 — is a hybrid reasoning model (streams a separate `reasoning` delta channel), but `isReasoningModel()` in `plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts` does not match it. Two consequences on a local Cerebras runtime (`ELIZA_PROVIDER=cerebras`, `OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL=zai-glm-4.7`):

1. The Cerebras-mode `reasoning_effort: "low"` default (added for exactly this failure on `gpt-oss-120b`) never applies to GLM, so its reasoning runs unbounded.
2. `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"` — set by core's planner loop (`packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts:1549`), Stage-1 formatting calls (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:8770`), and the app-model-tester — is silently ignored by plugin-openai, while plugin-elizacloud maps it to each Cerebras model's suppression value (`resolveCerebrasThinkingOffReasoningEffort`, `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts:366`).

With a capped `max_tokens` the model spends the entire budget on hidden reasoning and returns **empty visible content** with `finish_reason: "length"`.

## Reproduction (live Cerebras, wire-captured via a logging proxy)

`POST /api/model-tester/run {"test":"text-large"}` on a default local Cerebras setup, 8 runs. Every request went out as `{"model":"zai-glm-4.7","max_tokens":160}` with **no** `reasoning_effort`:

```
4x  finish_reason:"length"  reasoning_tokens:160/160  → "TEXT_LARGE returned empty text"
4x  finish_reason:"stop"    reasoning_tokens:62–157   → "pong"
```

~50% failure, purely dependent on how long GLM happens to reason. Direct curl confirms the mechanism: `reasoning_effort:"low"` bounds reasoning (~20–30 tokens), and `reasoning_effort:"none"` (accepted by gemma/GLM, rejected by gpt-oss) eliminates it entirely (`reasoning_tokens: 0`).

## Fix

PR incoming, mirroring plugin-elizacloud's settled semantics:

- add the GLM family to `isReasoningModel()` so the Cerebras `"low"` default applies;
- honor `eliza.thinking === "off"` in Cerebras mode by mapping it per model: `"none"` for gemma/GLM, `"low"` for gpt-oss; explicit caller `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` still wins.

After the fix (same wire capture): 10/10 probes return content, `finish_reason:"stop"`, `reasoning_tokens: 0`.

- 70 entries collide with files currently tracked by git; 9 have different bytes, including the LifeOps action manifest, three Eliza-1 smoke corpus splits, desktop/template app icons, and three homepage snapshots. A normal postinstall can therefore rewrite source after checkout.
- The archive is a competing binary authority for generated native/runtime outputs, including plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/verify/cuda_verify, an OS Bun executable, an old agent bundle, benchmark tools, and chip binaries. 1d:T592,Found while battery-testing every slash command through the GUI chat route (`POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/stream`).

**Repro:** in a conversation with enough history, send `/compact`. The deterministic command layer fires correctly (`thought: "Shortcut: cmd:compact"`), the COMPACT_CONVERSATION action succeeds, and its final text is the designed clean summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M."). But the **visible streamed reply** is the compactor's *internal* model call output — the raw rendered-ledger JSON:

```
```json
{ "state": { "facts": [ "…extracted facts from the conversation…" ] …
```

**Cause (as far as I traced):** the SSE route streams model tokens for the turn, and an action's *internal* `runtime.useModel` calls (here `buildActionCompactorModelCall`) stream into the same visible channel, so intermediate model output masquerades as the reply and the action's actual final text is buried. Any action that makes internal model calls on the GUI stream path has the same exposure. Discord/connector paths are unaffected (they deliver the discrete `reply` only).

This is the same family as the narration-envelope leaks (#8162): internal artifacts crossing into user-visible output. Expected behavior: mid-action internal model calls should not stream to the client; only the turn's actual reply should.

20:Tabe,**Describe the bug**

The resumable local-inference downloader cannot recover when a process exits after a bundle file has been fully written to its `.part` path but before the atomic rename into the bundle directory.

On restart, the sidecar SHA still matches the current manifest, so `resumableStartByte` returns the complete file size. The downloader sends `Range: bytes=<size>-`; a compliant server answers `416 Range Not Satisfiable`. The job fails while leaving the same complete partial in place, so every retry repeats the same 416. A valid multi-GB transfer is permanently stuck until the staging file is deleted manually.

**To Reproduce**

1. Put the complete expected bytes at the bundle file staging path.
2. Put the manifest SHA256 in the adjacent `.expected` sidecar.
3. Start the bundle download against an HTTP endpoint that returns 416 for a range beginning at EOF.
4. Observe the download fail before the existing SHA gate can commit the staged file.

Fails on current `develop` (`16a038447`):

<details><summary>RED test output</summary>

```text
FAIL local inference downloader stale-content robustness > commits a complete .part after restart without requesting a range past EOF
Error: HTTP 416 from model hub for elizaos/eliza-1/text/eliza-1-2b-128k.gguf
```

</details>

**Expected behavior**

Before issuing a Range request, a SHA-pinned complete partial should be recognized by its manifest SHA, atomically moved into the bundle, and have its `.expected` sidecar removed. Incomplete matching partials must continue to use normal Range resume.

Acceptance criteria:

- A complete SHA-matching `.part` is committed without another model-file request.
- The final file is byte-identical and the staging file/sidecar are gone.
- A partial strict prefix still resumes with `Range: bytes=N-`.
- Stale or unknown-provenance partials are still discarded.
- Downloader tests, plugin checks, and repository verification pass.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - downloader persistence failure has no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4/JPG: N/A - no rendered UI is involved.
- Backend logs: the exact failing consumer-boundary error is included above.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model call, prompt, action, or provider behavior is involved.
- Domain artifacts: complete `.part` plus matching `.expected` sidecar are created by the regression test.

**Additional context**

The existing final SHA256 gate prevents corrupt installation, so this is a crash-recovery/liveness bug rather than an integrity bypass. The failure window is narrow but expensive because bundle files are multi-gigabyte and the only current recovery is manual staging cleanup.21:T789,Found while live-proving #16171 on a running Discord agent. Three stacked gaps on the **plain-text command path** (native slash interactions are unaffected; webhooks/bots cannot fire those):

**1. Mention prefix defeats command detection.** A Discord message `<@bot> /model show` (strict-mention servers require the mention) reaches the pipeline with the mention still in `content.text`, so `hasCommand()` / the pre-LLM shortcut gate (which check `startsWith("/")`) never fire. The turn falls through to the planner. Bare `/model show` text works. Fix direction: strip the leading self-mention before command detection (connector-side normalization or in the gate's text extraction).

**2. The planner then improvises — including a hallucinated success claim.** Live repro: a **non-owner** webhook user sent `<@bot> /model coding codex gpt-5.4 low`. The write path held (config untouched — verified in eliza.json), but the bot replied:

A fabricated success claim for a mutation that never happened (same class as the phantom-claim rules from #7923/#7945).

**3. 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)22:Ta5d,## Summary

The `eliza-code-acp` first-use workspace provisioning path needs a deliberately designed cross-process build protocol. A proposed `O_EXCL` lock implementation was rejected during review and was **not** integrated because it still allowed concurrent/partial builds and broke paths containing spaces.

This is separate from the deterministic CI fix in #16167: mocked-native orchestrator tests now inject `ELIZA_ELIZAOS_ACP_COMMAND` and no longer enter real provisioning accidentally.

## Rejected implementation findings

- A hard-link/inode stale-reclaim check remained TOCTOU: two waiters could both validate stale lock A; waiter 1 could replace it with lock B; waiter 2 could then delete B using its already-authorized unlink.
- A wall-clock stale threshold could steal from a verified-live owner. `spawnSync()` may remain blocked after its timeout if the child traps `SIGTERM`, so age alone cannot prove ownership ended. PID reuse also makes liveness-only checks ambiguous.
- The mtime fast path could bypass a still-held lock as soon as a partial `dist/acp.js` appeared. A crash after touching/truncating the file could leave a permanently false-fresh artifact.
- The generated native command was a single unquoted string; `splitCommandLine()` breaks Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## Required design properties

- [ ] Deterministic multi-process exclusion with fencing or an equivalent proven primitive.
- [ ] No stealing from a verified-live owner based only on wall-clock age.
- [ ] Replacement-lock-safe cleanup; an old owner/waiter can never delete a successor's lock.
- [ ] Build to a temporary artifact, validate it, and publish atomically under the lock (or use an equivalent completion marker tied to the artifact).
- [ ] Crash/dead-owner recovery without PID-reuse ambiguity.
- [ ] One shared deadline across waiting and building, with bounded status/signal/stdout/stderr diagnostics.
- [ ] Structured `{ command, args }` propagation, or equivalently robust path handling, including workspaces/Bun binaries with spaces.

## Required tests

- [ ] Multiple processes reclaim one dead lock; exactly one fake build runs.
- [ ] A holder writes `partial` while keeping the lock; a waiter cannot return until the validated complete artifact is atomically published.
- [ ] A live owner beyond the nominal wait budget is never overlapped.
- [ ] Crash recovery and PID-reuse simulation.
- [ ] Workspace and Bun paths containing spaces.
- [ ] Failed build never leaves a fresh-looking executable artifact.

Do not solve this by increasing timeouts or reintroducing test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.

- Runtime proof: full `packages/core` suite and focused integration results will be attached to the PR.30:T975,## Problem

`RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT` can report a different Claude/Codex model than `plugin-cli-inference` actually invokes.

PR #16310 added static `displayModel` metadata by reading `process.env` when the plugin module is constructed. The handlers, however, resolve model names from `runtime.getSetting()` for each agent and tier:

- Claude large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_MODEL`
- Claude planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting
- Codex large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_MODEL`
- Codex planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting

Core deliberately gives per-agent runtime settings precedence and does not read `process.env` from `getSetting()`. In a multi-agent process, or whenever a character setting differs from the host environment, the handler calls one model while its context provider claims another. The new unit tests only exercise environment-derived metadata and therefore do not cover the real runtime boundary.

## Required fix

- Make model-registration display metadata able to resolve an ordered list of runtime setting keys before a static provider default.
- Declare CLI-inference metadata per tier:
  - large tiers: the backend's large-model runtime key, then its canonical default;
  - planner: planner-model runtime key, then large-model runtime key, then the canonical default.
- Preserve serializable, handler-free registration metadata.
- Add integration coverage that constructs a real `AgentRuntime` with runtime values intentionally conflicting with `process.env`, registers the plugin models, invokes `RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT`, and proves the reported model matches the handler's effective model for both a large slot and the planner fallback chain.
- Keep inert/native-planner behavior unchanged.
- Replace change-narration comments with durable system rationale per the repository comment policy.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Per-agent/character settings override host environment values in model self-reporting.
- [ ] Planner metadata follows the same planner -> large -> default precedence as the handler.
- [ ] Claude and Codex backends are covered.
- [ ] Existing core and plugin-cli-inference tests pass.
- [ ] `bun run verify` passes on a branch rebased onto current `develop`.
- [ ] Exact-head evidence includes the provider output and handler-selected model from a real runtime, manually reviewed and attached inline to the PR.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes release tooling and the manifest preparation order used by the release workflow. Failure modes are intentionally stricter: malformed workspace metadata, ambiguous registry responses, changed manifests, conflicting integrity, moved Git refs, or inconsistent retry evidence now halt the release. The new unit and real-process integration suites cover successful publication, failure before mutation, partial publication, exact-integrity resume, registry restart, atomic Git rejection, ref movement, and idempotent retries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This adds the fail-closed, immutable release primitives requested by #16276:

- A canonical, deterministic release contract records the source/expected commit, exact version and channel, explicit package cohort and directories, dependency graph and order, manifest identity, entrypoint metadata, and the real packed tarball path, size, SHA-512 digest, SRI integrity, and npm shasum. `2.0.3-beta.8`, `.9`, and `.10` remain permanently reserved.
- One shared workspace resolver now serves release scripts and app-core discovery. Invalid manifests, non-file manifests, duplicate package names, and unexpected filesystem failures surface instead of disappearing.
- Candidate creation requires a clean exact Git HEAD, executes one caller-supplied build exactly once, verifies manifests did not change, invokes `npm pack --json --ignore-scripts` once per package, validates the packed package identity and entrypoints, and records immutable real `.tgz` artifacts. Candidate state uses canonical JSON, ordered phase transitions, atomic replacement, and an exclusive writer lock.
- Registry inspection treats only HTTP 404 as missing. Authentication, authorization, throttling, server, transport, redirect, and malformed-response failures are distinct hard errors. Publication preflights the complete cohort before mutation, skips only exact-integrity matches, resumes missing packages from the recorded tarballs, promotes the requested channel only after full verification, and binds retries to the recorded registry.
- Git publication pushes only the explicitly named branch and tag in one atomic operation with an exact old-branch lease. Remote movement, non-fast-forward updates, conflicting tags, retry evidence changes, and reserved tags fail without mutation; matching annotated or lightweight tags dereference to the expected commit and no-op.
- `replace-workspace-versions.js`, `restore-workspace-refs.js`, and `release-set-public-access.mjs` now delegate to shared transactional manifest primitives. Rewrites use exact target versions, preserve byte-exact originals in an integrity journal, roll back on write/journal failure, and refuse restoration over a changed manifest.
- The only release workflow behavior change is the required access-before-reference-journal ordering, so the journal snapshot and packed manifests describe the same final bytes. The broader `release.yaml` orchestration remains outside this primitive-focused issue.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and non-breaking release-infrastructure feature.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was required and is included in `.github/workflows/README.md`, covering the immutable artifact/state contract, exact resume rules, reserved versions, registry/Git safety properties, commands, and the deliberate orchestration boundary.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with these executable specifications:

1. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-verdaccio.integration.test.ts` for a real local registry failure and integrity-matched resume.
2. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-git.integration.test.ts` for real bare-Git atomic ref publication and rejection.
3. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-contract.test.ts` for cohort, dependency graph, exact-version, reserved-version, deterministic serialization, and state-machine invariants.
4. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-candidate.test.ts` and `release-manifests.test.ts` for immutable `.tgz` creation and transactional manifest behavior.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run install:light

bun test --coverage \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/workspaces-lib.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-contract.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-candidate.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-manifests.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-registry.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-verdaccio.integration.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-git.integration.test.ts

bun run verify
bun run audit:error-policy-ratchet

[sol-cloud]36:T15e6,# Relates to

Relates to #16277.

Dependency #16276 is already merged through #16285. This branch is rebased directly onto that prerequisite at `42838644ed2c871916a1151db145825a1234497d`.

# Outcome

Replaces the legacy single-job release script with an explicit three-stage transaction:

1. A credential-free candidate job verifies the exact repository/ref/SHA, builds and packs the reviewed cohort once, and records immutable plan, cohort, manifest, entrypoint, and tarball integrity.
2. A least-privilege npm job authenticates the planned publisher, binds the normalized registry before mutation, stages missing immutable versions under a cohort-derived candidate tag, verifies the full cohort, promotes the public channel, and removes the staging tags.
3. A finalizer re-reads npm without credentials, binds the Git push destination, pushes only the exact planned annotated tag, and creates or verifies a GitHub Release whose repository, tag, target commit, and prerelease identity match the plan.

Every mutation command requires the complete release identity plus the canonical plan digest. Exclusive state locks and monotonic `registry-bound` / `git-bound` transitions make interruption retries idempotent without permitting a target, publisher, candidate-tag, ref, or artifact swap.

# Pack-once invariant

The obsolete `verify-npm-pack-dist.mjs` helper is removed because its only caller was the legacy workflow and it rebuilt plus dry-packed packages a second time. Validation is not reduced: candidate construction now performs the single real `npm pack`, checks the actual `.tgz` bytes, verifies every declared `main` / `module` / `types` / `bin` / concrete `exports` target is present, records SHA-512 hex and SRI, and revalidates the same immutable tarballs before every side effect. Removing the legacy helper is what preserves the issue's build/test/pack-once contract and clears the script-orphan audit.

# Risk and rollout

Risk is high because this is public release infrastructure. The workflow remains manual/reusable, uses one global non-cancelling concurrency group, reserves failed beta versions, separates trusted tooling from candidate source, and never pushes a branch. This PR did not contact public npm for publication or create/update any public Git tag or GitHub Release.

38:T1a40,"## Problem\n\nNothing deterministic stood between a sub-agent's \"done\" claim and the task being declared done:\n\n- **No residuals check existed anywhere** — no `git status --porcelain`, no unpushed-commit check; \"tests pass / no leftovers\" was decided by an LLM reading self-reported envelopes and regex-mined transcript text.\n- `validateTask` wrote `status:\"done\"` **directly**, bypassing the lifecycle transition table; `humanOverride` could promote a task from ANY status with a fabricated default evidence string, via plain `POST /tasks/:id/validate`.\n- Operator paths (`retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent`/`restartTask`/`reopenTask`) also bypassed the table, contradicting the table module's \"single legal way\" contract.\n- The user-facing completion relay never consulted verification state — the user could be told \"done\" while verification was still running or failing.\n- Criteria-free tasks skipped verification entirely; a dead duplicate of the goal-contract module diverged from the live one.\n\n## Fix\n\n- New `completion-residuals.ts` — deterministic gate run **before any model spend** in `autoVerifyCompletion` and before honoring `passed:true` in `validateTask`: real `git status --porcelain`, `git rev-list @{u}..HEAD` (when an upstream exists), envelope legs (`testResults[].exitCode !== 0`, non-empty `residualRisks`). Statuses: `clean | residuals | unverifiable`. Fail-closed (`unverifiable`) when the task **expects a repo** (`session.repo` or `task.boundRepo`); for unbound tasks the git legs run opportunistically when the workdir is a real git worktree and skip otherwise (every ACP session has a scratch workdir — a voice/Q&A task must not be blocked by a non-git scratch dir; caught by merged-tree scenario verification). Default ON; `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_RESIDUALS_GATE=0` disables.\n- Blocked completions re-engage the worker through the existing `reEngageOrEscalate` loop with a correction enumerating the exact residuals, bounded by `MAX_AUTO_VERIFY_ATTEMPTS`, parking `waiting_on_user` at cap. The snapshot is persisted on task metadata + validation events so the UI shows what blocked (or was overridden at) completion.\n- **All durable status writes now route through the transition table**: new `human_override_passed/failed` triggers (non-terminal states only), `restarted` made total; `retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent` via `retrying`, `restartTask`/`reopenTask` via `restarted`/`reopened`. [sol-cloud]
3c:T103b,## What

Makes the workspace-native **eliza-code ACP** first-use provisioning path (`ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` in `plugin-agent-orchestrator`) crash-safe and multi-process safe, per the design properties in #16169.

Previously `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` built `dist/acp.js` with a naive missing-or-stale check and no cross-process coordination:
- concurrent first spawns could interleave writes and publish a corrupt executable,
- a crash after `bun build` truncated the artifact left a partial `acp.js` whose mtime looked fresh forever,
- the launch command was an unquoted string that `splitCommandLine` broke on Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## How

New `acp-provisioning.ts` implements the deterministic protocol required by the issue. `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` becomes a thin adapter over it, preserving the existing string-command contract (byte-identical for quote/space-free paths).

- **Fenced mutual exclusion** — lock created with `open(..., "wx")` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL)`; record carries `{pid, fence, startedAtMs}`. Ownership is the fence token, never age or PID alone.
- **No stealing from a verified-live owner** — a waiter reclaims only when the owner PID is not alive; a dead owner is reclaimed immediately (crash recovery within the outer deadline).
- **PID-reuse disambiguation** — a "live" PID older than an absolute lifetime ceiling is treated as reused and reclaimed, resolving the ambiguity the issue calls out.
- **Single-winner reclaim** — an `O_EXCL` reclaim-intent gate ensures exactly one reclaimer replaces a stale lock and runs the build at a time (the shared `bun run build` canonical output is not safe to run concurrently). The intent records its holder PID so a crashed holder is cleared immediately, not after the ceiling.
- **Gap-free lock replacement** — reclaim installs the new lock via a temp+atomic-`rename`, so `paths.lock` is never momentarily absent (no window for a third `open("wx")` acquirer).
- **Atomic publish + completion marker** — build writes to a private per-fence temp, is validated (non-empty, marker present), then `rename`d into place; a `dist/.acp.done` marker records the published size+mtime. Freshness is keyed on the marker, so a bare/partial `acp.js` is never treated as fresh and a failed/invalid build never leaves a fresh-looking artifact.
- **Space/quote-safe command** — `formatAcpCommand` quotes only when needed and picks the quote char the path lacks, so paths with spaces or quotes round-trip through `splitCommandLine` (which has no escape grammar). Quote-free paths stay bare (unchanged behavior).

## Tests

`acp-provisioning.test.ts` (24 tests) simulates crash-at-each-step via injectable `build` / `isPidAlive` / `now` hooks and covers every required scenario:
- multiple processes reclaim one dead lock, exactly one build runs;
- a partial/unmarked artifact is never fresh; a failed/invalid build leaves no fresh artifact;
- a verified-live owner is never overlapped; crash + PID-reuse recovery;
- malformed-lock recovery (crash mid-claim) with a mid-claim grace window;
- single-winner reclaim: a held gate blocks a second reclaimer; a dead-holder gate is cleared immediately; an aged-out gate is recovered;
- gap-free lock install; overlapping builds never corrupt/partially publish (atomic-publish invariant);
- space- and quote-containing paths round-trip through the command.

**Evidence (VPS, bun canary):**
- `acp-provisioning.test.ts` + `eliza-code-acp-install.test.ts` + existing `acp-service.test.ts`: **82 passed**.
- Coverage on `acp-provisioning.ts`: **~85% lines / ~80% branch / 95% funcs** (>= 50% bar).
- `tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors. Biome: clean. `error-policy-ratchet` vs `origin/develop`: `emptyCatch 0->0`, no new fallback-slop.
- Codex review (`gpt-5.5`) against the commit: no findings after iterating through the P1/P2 races it surfaced (successor race, PID reuse, shared canonical build output, intent-timeout-vs-deadline, error-policy annotations) — all fixed in this branch.

Does not increase timeouts and does not reintroduce test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.

Closes #16169

# Risk

The synthetic corpus exercises mechanics only and is explicitly excluded from model-quality and release evidence.

# What changed

- The builder serializes only the canonical formatter/privacy-filter result; raw source records cannot cross the output boundary.
- The tracked corpus is 20 source-controlled synthetic rows: 16 train, 2 validation, and 2 test, spanning direct, multi-turn, native-boundary, tool-call, and legacy formats.
- Manifest revision 3 records exact generator, formatter, privacy filter, source, split hashes, counts, and bytes. `--check` proves byte-for-byte regeneration and fails closed when Git provenance is unavailable.
- Privacy handling and emitted-byte tests cover credentials, contacts, numeric latitude/longitude, nested GeoJSON coordinates, case variants, and duplicate longitude aliases.
- `train_local.py` rejects malformed JSON, incompatible rows, invalid tool arguments, non-finite/negative controls, and partial preflight success. Its checkpoint scan covers actual shard patterns, nested tensors, and empty scans.
- Release evaluation no longer auto-selects the synthetic smoke corpus. Missing held-out data is publish-blocking, and results carry corpus path, hash, and record count.
- RL/Atropos paths share runtime state-root precedence, including `ELIZA_TRAJECTORY_DIR`; dead literal fields and swallowed curriculum persistence failures are removed.
- The training workflow runs on pull requests, keeps PR-controlled code off persistent self-hosted runners, and executes builder/privacy/validator/low-VRAM/Stage-2/scenario/evaluation suites.
- Tracked pytest output, mutable curriculum state, stale sidecars, and obsolete release-queue evidence are removed.

3e:T1def,Relates to #16278

## Summary

- remove the startup-failing `release-all.yml` aggregate without changing `release.yaml`, the merged immutable release primitives, or the transactional npm work in #16277
- remove its unused `flatpak-publish.yml` child and the credential playbook for a Flathub repository that does not exist
- preserve the standalone OS manifest recovery operation by replacing its fail-open reusable/direct-push design with a manual-only, exact-SHA-bound, strict-checksum, pull-request boundary
- make the workflow catalog and OS secret map describe the real call graph, including distinct Snap credentials and the retained Snap/APT skip-as-success defect owned by #16279
- replace name/literal-only assertions with a real-tree YAML contract covering all local reusable calls, automatic versus manual manifest writers, recovery permissions/triggers/commands, the known automatic OS permission rejection, and actual orchestrated secret identities

## Root cause and correction

`release-all.yml` combined a second release coordinator, OS artifact publication, store dispatches, and an npm event-chain assumption in one entry point. Its entire recorded history is 13 `startup_failure` runs with zero jobs. Its `GITHUB_TOKEN`-created GitHub release also could not trigger `release.yaml`, because repository-token events do not recursively start another workflow except explicit dispatch events.

The first version of this PR incorrectly treated `update-os-release-manifest.yml` as behaviorally redundant. Exact-head review showed that it uniquely accepted a manifest/tag for already-published assets while the retained full OS workflow has only startup failures. This revision preserves that recovery behavior but removes its failure masking and direct protected-branch push: the operator must pin the current `origin/develop` SHA and release-tag commit SHA; every release asset is downloaded and checksummed; strict validation and checksum verification must pass; and the only mutation is a dedicated branch plus reviewable PR.

## Sync with develop

- [x] Final head is `fad8d2bea3b23c5ce779666ee0426e858c127a67`.
- [x] `bun run verify` passed post-rebase and post-commit.

## Authority evidence

### Proven removals

| Entry point | Repository evidence | GitHub history |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `release-all.yml` | Competing aggregate; relied on a non-recursive `GITHUB_TOKEN` release event to reach npm | 13/13 `startup_failure`, every run with zero jobs; [latest run](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/27886935066) |
| `flatpak-publish.yml` | Sole in-repo caller was `release-all.yml`; target `flathub/ai.elizaos.App` is absent | zero recorded runs |

The structural scan finds no remaining reference to either retired workflow, no `FLATHUB_TOKEN` consumer, and no local reusable call whose target is missing or lacks `workflow_call`.

### Preserved manual recovery

```text
update-os-release-manifest.yml
  trigger: workflow_dispatch only
  required identity: manifest path + release tag + current develop SHA + tag commit SHA
  data: exact GitHub release asset set
  checks: canonical checksum regeneration + strict manifest validation + checksum verification
  mutation: unique recovery branch + pull request to develop
  forbidden: workflow_call, release/push trigger, direct develop/main push
```

The automatic/manual writer inventory is exactly:

```text
automatic: elizaos-os-full-release.yml
manual recovery: update-os-release-manifest.yml
```

This is an authority inventory, not a health claim. The test detects that the automatic OS caller's read-only ceiling cannot satisfy the retained reusable children’s `contents`, `id-token`, `attestations`, and `pages` write requests. The catalog therefore says explicitly that the automatic path is startup-invalid and routes its repair to #16279.

### Secret/call graph correction

`release-orchestrator.yml` calls `publish-packages.yml` with PyPI, Snap, APT, and Flatpak enabled. That child reads `PYPI_API_TOKEN`, `SNAP_STORE_CREDENTIALS`, and `APT_REPO_TOKEN`. The separate `snap-publish.yml` reads `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS`. The checklist now records both paths and warns that missing orchestrated Snap/APT credentials can still skip the store mutation while the containing build succeeds; #16279 owns that completion repair.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Resolves one safe application trace id from `X-Eliza-Trace-Id`, strict W3C v00 `traceparent`, or a generated UUID and records it in Worker request telemetry.
- Emits browser-readable `Server-Timing` plus `X-Eliza-Preforward-Ms` for monotonic `auth`, `middle`, `reserve`, `setup`, and `total` gateway phases.
- Freezes that timing at the actual `fetch` / `streamText` / `generateText` invocation after request and provider configuration is built, then logs and returns the same two-decimal snapshot.
- Preserves timing on provider failures, immutable streaming responses, and `/v1/responses` success/remapped-5xx compatibility responses without buffering the body.
- Merges telemetry CORS exposure after route handlers so existing x402/payment exposure headers are not overwritten.
- Finalizes in-memory request telemetry when a handler throws and records the trace id on both success and error paths.
- Explicitly enables complete Worker logs and 5% native trace sampling in Wrangler.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and observability improvements (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is required for this correctness slice. The full deployment topology and remaining end-to-end telemetry work are documented in #16079 and #16085.

42:T10af,## What

Corrective follow-up to the prematurely merged #16293. This closes #16300 by making the Codex one-time refresh-token path fail closed from selection through the periodic account sweep.

- Codex selection reconciles every materialized token generation under the account refresh mutex before any canonical token resolution. Adoption failure propagates; neither `select()` nor the keep-alive sweep can fall through to `getAccessToken` with the consumed token.
- A published `auth.json` is thereafter Codex-owned. The bridge creates a refresh-token-addressed generation with atomic no-replace publication and never overwrites an existing generation, so an external Codex truncate/write cannot lose the only valid rotated pair in an adoption/write gap.
- Stable stat/read/stat retries cover the real truncate-then-write behavior of the external Codex process. Divergent generations require a real parseable `last_refresh`; ambiguous order fails instead of guessing.
- Initial materialization uses the canonical record's observable `updatedAt`, not materialization time, so it cannot invent a fresher OAuth generation.
- `config.toml` and the non-secret `active-home` pointer are atomically replaced and verified. Failure to pin `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` aborts the spawn.
- The benchmark adapter follows the validated active generation while preserving the pre-generation account-root layout.

## Correctness boundary

The in-process account mutex serializes reconciliation, canonical persistence, config publication, and selection. An external Codex process cannot honor that mutex, so the bridge removes the conflicting write entirely: after the initial no-replace publish, only Codex writes that generation. A later OAuth login gets a different generation path; reconciliation scans both without clobbering either.

43:T1058,Relates to #15347, #16194, #16079, and #16228.

## What changed

- routes every Cloudflare Worker-to-agent API, bridge, SSE, workflow, wallet, LifeOps, snapshot, and restore request through the configured control-plane origin
- carries the logical `<agent-id>.<agent-domain>` identity in trusted `X-Forwarded-Host` / `X-Forwarded-Proto` headers so the control-plane router selects the intended agent
- validates the control-plane host, base domain, agent UUID, and relative path before network I/O
- strips caller-controlled host, forwarding, and auth headers; restores the stored agent credential; and disables redirects so custom auth headers cannot follow a cross-origin redirect
- fails closed before fetch when routing configuration or the stored agent credential is missing
- preserves the existing direct tailnet/public routing behavior outside Cloudflare Workers
- declares the production default origin while retaining explicit staging and production environment bindings

## Why

The real managed-agent canary provisioned a healthy dedicated agent, but the nested same-zone Worker fetch to its UUID wildcard hostname failed at Cloudflare with HTTP 525 before nginx, the router, authentication, Headscale, or the model runtime received HTTP. Worker subrequests must target the fixed control-plane origin and pass the logical agent hostname separately.

## Validation

- exact head: `1a8277c542d5e90c58ec6ea61587f95036718a70`
- seven changed sandbox suites: 141 passed, 0 failed, 609 assertions
- `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck`
- `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/api typecheck` (includes production Worker bundle dry-run)
- explicit staging Wrangler dry-run confirms `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST=eliza-staging-1.elizacloud.ai`
- production dry-run confirms `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST=eliza-production-1.elizacloud.ai`
- Biome, `git diff --check`, and `bun run audit:error-policy-ratchet`

## Deployment and post-merge acceptance

The repository's canonical Cloud deployment guard intentionally rejects feature refs, so post-fix infrastructure evidence cannot be captured from this branch. After merge, deploy this exact change to staging through the canonical `develop` workflow, then run #16228's managed dedicated canary and require successful bridge, SSE, real model turn, exact cleanup, and deployed-commit provenance before production promotion.

## Risk

Medium: this centralizes all Worker-side dedicated-agent calls. Invalid configuration now fails closed instead of falling back to the broken wildcard route. No database migration or public API shape changes are included.

44:T1405,"## Problem\n\nWith per-task GROUP rooms on by default, the ACP session's `metadata.roomId` is the minted task-room UUID while the user types in the origin connector channel. Every consumer that read the raw `roomId` diverged from the router's origin ladder:\n\n- **Mid-task user follow-ups were silently never forwarded** to the running sub-agent — `boundToRoom` in `active-session-forward.ts` matched only `meta.roomId`/`meta.threadRoomId`, neither of which is the origin room once a task room is minted.\n- **Live progress (acks/heartbeats/narration) targeted the minted GROUP room**, which is not a live connector channel; sends failed and were demoted to debug after the first warn — the user often saw nothing between spawn and completion.\n- **`QUESTION_FOR_TASK_CREATOR` landed only in the task room** — a blocked sub-agent's question never directly reached the user.\n- Multi-session rooms forwarded only to the **first** bound session; inbox overflow **silently dropped** the oldest queued message; and a bind-retry-window race between `SubAgentRouter` and `SwarmCoordinatorService` could double-post or lose a completion.\n\n## Fix\n\n- New `session-room-binding.ts`: shared origin ladder (`resolveOriginRoomId`) + bound-room set (`sessionBoundRoomIds`) used by the forward handler and the progress hook, with an anti-drift contract test pinning agreement with the router's `readOrigin` across all metadata shapes.\n- Forward handler binds on the full session room set (task/origin/source/thread) and delivers to **all** bound live sessions with a per-session interruption decision.\n- Progress hook resolves emit targets through the origin ladder, so acks/heartbeats/narration reach the user's actual channel.\n- `QUESTION_FOR_TASK_CREATOR` routes to BOTH the task room (planner context) and the origin room (user-facing leg), deduped when they are equal.\n- `SubAgentInbox` overflow surfaces dropped counts via an observer wired to `logger.warn` + `runtime.reportError` — no silent drops.\n- Swarm coordinator cede decision uses a **receipt-time router-activity snapshot** instead of a post-await `isActive()` read. (Ceding to a still-binding router would lose the event outright — ACP fan-out has no replay — so the snapshot closes the double-post race in both directions; regression tests cover bind-during-gap and unbind-during-gap.)\n\n## Evidence\n\n- Targeted suites (6 files): **191/191 passed**. Full plugin unit lane: **141 files / 1578 tests passed**.\n- Merged-integration check (this branch + `fix/orchestrator-completion-gating` + `test/orchestrator-delarp-scenarios` on develop): full orchestrator suite **201 files / 2142 tests passed**; pr-deterministic scenario lane **13/13 passed**, including the new `orchestrator-follow-up-forwarding` (origin-room follow-up delivered/queued/ignored matrix) and `orchestrator-question-for-creator` scenarios that drive the real forward handler and real router.\n- `bun run audit:error-policy-ratchet`: no new fallback-slop in touched files. Biome clean.\n- Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — no prompt/model-behavior change; routing/plumbing only, covered by deterministic scenario drives of the real router/forwarder.\n- Screenshots/video: N/A — no UI surface changed.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\\n\\n## Evidence Gate\\n\\n<!-- evidence-row:before-screenshots -->\\n- [x] Before screenshots: N/A - backend orchestrator routing only; no rendered UI changed.\\n<!-- evidence-row:after-screenshots -->\\n- [x] After screenshots: N/A - backend orchestrator routing only; no rendered UI changed.\\n<!-- evidence-row:walkthrough-video -->\\n- [x] Walkthrough video: N/A - no visual interaction changed.\\n<!-- evidence-row:backend-logs -->\\n- [x] Backend logs: [reviewed focused Vitest report](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence/pr16254-orchestrator-focused-vitest.json) records 197/197 passing route, forwarding, inbox, question, and coordinator assertions.\\n<!-- evidence-row:frontend-logs -->\\n- [x] Frontend console/network logs: N/A - no frontend or network client code changed.\\n<!-- evidence-row:llm-trajectory -->\\n- [x] Real-LLM trajectory: [exact OpenAI interruption-controller prompts and responses](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence/pr16255-routing-live-model-io.jsonl) prove unrelated reminder -> ignore, idle task follow-up -> deliver, and mid-turn task follow-up -> queue.\\n<!-- evidence-row:domain-artifacts -->\\n- [x] Domain artifacts: [live scenario matrix](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence/pr16254-55-live-matrix.json) records 2/2 passed, including origin-channel classification; the focused report above proves the deterministic real-router and persistence invariants.\\n\\nThe live scenario artifacts came from [workflow run 29226497574](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29226497574). - [x] Post-sync `bun run verify` passed: 573/573 Turbo tasks plus every follow-on repository audit.

# Background

A clean boot could report settled health after service startup failed. The failure was logged and converted to `null`, late plugin schema work could race service startup, and scheduling seeded tasks before its runner was registered. Those independent paths made broken initialization look healthy.

This change makes boot state transactional and observable:

- Service startup failures are typed, reported with plugin/service context, and retained in structured boot health.
- Handler and service registration roll back when startup/finalization fails.
- Late plugin registration serializes schema work before starting dependent services.
- Scheduling registers its runner structurally before the seed service runs.
- Personal-assistant scheduling contributions use the shared scheduling service boundary.
- Health and readiness distinguish pending, successful, and failed service initialization.

# Risks

Medium-high. This changes core plugin/service lifecycle ordering and intentionally turns formerly hidden initialization failures into unhealthy readiness. The added rollback and real PGlite integration coverage protect the affected paths, but final packaged fresh-state boot logs remain an explicit draft gate.

# Testing

A reviewer can start with:

```bash

```

46:T107c,Closes #16253.

## What changed

- Atomically claims a CLI key reveal so concurrent pollers have exactly one plaintext winner.
- Returns typed reveal outcomes instead of conflating an expected losing poll with missing or corrupt durable state.
- Throws `CLI_AUTH_SESSION_INTEGRITY` for missing references, owner mismatches, and incomplete encrypted key material.
- Revalidates key ID, user, organization, active/deleted/expiry state, and key hash inside the consume update.
- Rejects a candidate invalidated by the real API-key update writer during KMS decryption.
- Keeps expected losing callers plaintext-free and leaves KMS failures retryable.
- Covers the legacy `/api/v1/cli-auth/:session/token` boundary so every changed production source participates in the enforced coverage gate.

The browser completion endpoint returns only `keyPrefix`; plaintext is disclosed only by the single-use CLI poll boundary.

48:T1e72,## What

Under Eliza Cloud routing (`serviceRouting.llmText` = `cloud-proxy`/`elizacloud`), the model-config surface read and wrote the wrong env-key family. `CHAT_PROVIDER_KEY_FAMILY` mapped `elizacloud → OPENAI`, but the plugin-collector removes plugin-openai entirely under cloud-proxy — so `/model large …` wrote `OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL`, a key cloud chat never reads, and `/model show` displayed a stale pin "via api.cerebras.ai" while inference actually ran through elizacloud.ai. Every fresh cloud dedicated agent hits this.

### Fixes
- **`ELIZAOS_CLOUD` key family**: `elizacloud` writes `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_{SMALL,LARGE}_MODEL` + `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_REASONING_EFFORT`; GET reports those keys (with the plugin's code defaults, source `default`, when cloud is the active brain).
- **`activeChat` on GET `/api/models/config`**: `{provider, family, endpoint}` resolved from the serviceRouting topology — the same signal the plugin-collector uses — so operator surfaces name what ACTUALLY serves.
- **Ambiguity resolves to the active provider**: the Cerebras trio is offered by both `cerebras` and `elizacloud`; a bare `/model small gemma-4-31b` now lands on whichever brain serves chat instead of 400ing.
- **Effort knob wired end-to-end**: plugin-elizacloud consumes `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_REASONING_EFFORT` (`reasoning_effort` on the wire), scoped to the Cerebras-served reasoning trio; the thinking-off suppression still wins.
- **Renderer + UI follow the active family**: `/model show` prefers the active family's keys and shows the true endpoint (effort must come from the same family as the model it annotates); `useModelConfiguration` maps each provider to its real key family and reads the elizacloud prefill from `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_*`.

### Defaults refresh (per product direction)
- codex coding: powerful `gpt-5.6-sol`, fast `gpt-5.6-luna` (was terra/5.4-mini) — `TASK_AGENT_DEFAULT_MODEL_PREFS`, `CODING_MODEL_DEFAULTS`, codex `config.toml` fallback, all mirrored.
- claude coding: fast `claude-sonnet-5` added (powerful stays `claude-opus-4-8`); `claude-fable-5` confirmed selectable in chat + coding catalogs.
- Eliza Cloud large: `DEFAULT_ELIZA_CLOUD_LARGE_TEXT_MODEL = zai-glm-4.7` (core + shared contracts) — large is now genuinely larger than small; first-run large/mega tiers use it.
- Stale ids: provider-switch stamps `claude-opus-4-8` / `gpt-5.4-mini` / `gemini-2.5-pro` (these env stamps override plugin defaults for anyone who used the switch UI); plugin-openai large `gpt-5` → `gpt-5.5`; plugin-google-genai `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-03-25` → `gemini-2.5-pro`; media-provider vision `gpt-4o` → `gpt-5-mini`.

4e:Tfc8,## Summary

Fixes the staging sign-in regression Shadow hit **2026-07-12 01:33 MDT**: navigating the cli-login page rendered **"Authentication Error — Session already authenticated or expired"** with a "Sign In Again" button, even though the same flow had worked ~40 min earlier (he was in the app at 22:18).

**Root cause (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/cli-auth-sessions.ts:77`):** `completeAuthentication` treated **any** non-`pending` session status as an error. The browser cli-login page (`packages/ui/.../auth/cli-login-page.tsx`) POSTs `/api/auth/cli-session/:id/complete` inside a React effect that can fire **more than once** for the same session:

- React StrictMode double-invoke,
- an effect re-run when `ready`/`authenticated` transition,
- a retry, or
- the user **revisiting the same `?session=` URL** within the 10-minute TTL.

The **first** POST flips the session `pending → authenticated` and mints the CLI API key (this is why sign-in worked at 22:18). A **second** POST then hit `session.status !== "pending"` → threw `"Session already authenticated or expired"` → HTTP 400 → the page rendered the hard error panel. The user *was* authenticated; the flow just wasn't idempotent.

### Fresh regression or latent flake?

**Latent flake.** `git log origin/develop` shows **no** commits touching the cli-session route / service / schema since 2026-07-10. The double-consumption path has existed since the redirect logic landed in **#15617 (2026-07-08)**. Not a tonight-deploy regression.

**KMS ruled out:** the KMS/api-key mint path is only exercised on the *first* (pending) completion, which succeeded (Shadow reached the app). The error string is uniquely emitted by the status guard, never by key minting. `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=local` (set 07-11) is not implicated.

## Fix

`completeAuthentication` is now **idempotent**:

- **Already authenticated by the SAME user** → returns success (`alreadyAuthenticated: true`) **without minting a second key**, echoing `keyPrefix` from the existing `api_keys` row. (The CLI/device receives the plaintext key via the separate single-use poll endpoint; the browser only reads `keyPrefix`.)
- **Authenticated by a DIFFERENT user** → still rejected (no session leak).
- **Expired / invalid** → still the clear error.

The complete route now returns `alreadyAuthenticated` in its 200 body. The browser page already redirects app-launched sessions to `returnTo` on success, so an idempotent re-completion now lands the user back in the app instead of dead-ending on the error panel.

## Tests (13, all green)

**`packages/cloud/shared/.../cli-auth-sessions.idempotent-complete.test.ts` (5, bun:test):**
- pending session mints a key, `alreadyAuthenticated=false` (negative control)
- same-user idempotent re-complete: success, **no second key minted**
- missing `api_keys` row: success with null keyPrefix
- different-user session: rejected (no leak)
- missing/expired session: clear error

**`packages/cloud/api/auth/cli-session/[sessionId]/complete/route.test.ts` (4, bun:test):**
- fresh completion → 200
- **REGRESSION**: idempotent re-POST → **200** (not the old 400)
- expired/other-user → 400 clear error
- invalid session → 400

**`packages/ui/.../auth/cli-login-page.test.tsx` (+2, vitest):**
- idempotent already-authenticated success still **redirects to `returnTo`** (Shadow's exact `app-staging.elizacloud.ai/chat` case) — never the error panel
- idempotent success with no `returnTo` lands terminal success screen

```
cloud/shared idempotent-complete: 5 pass
cloud/api complete route:         4 pass
ui cli-login-page:               13 pass (11 existing + 2 new)
```

## ⚠️ Auth surface — flagged for human review

This changes API-key minting / session-completion semantics. Please review the idempotency guard (same-user check, no-double-mint) and the added `alreadyAuthenticated` response field.

## What changed

- replaced synthetic room maps and runtime spies with real `AgentRuntime` instances backed by `InMemoryDatabaseAdapter`
- made channel-topic hydration and persistence failures throw typed `ElizaError` values with their adapter cause, report through `runtime.reportError`, and leave failed hydration retryable
- renders an explicit `# Current topics unavailable` provider state when persistence cannot be loaded, distinct from a legitimately empty topic LRU
- preserves the designed no-op when a room disappears during a deletion race
- added an exact-head credentialed lane that persists two topics, composes the real `CHANNEL_TOPICS` provider state, and sends that state through the configured live text model

## Outcome

Opus 4.7 and 4.8 now use provider-managed adaptive thinking across native Anthropic, OpenRouter, and Vercel AI Gateway resolution. The serialized request never sends a manual thinking token budget for those models. Opus through 4.6 and Sonnet 4 retain their existing `enabled + budgetTokens` behavior, and a zero/absent configured budget still disables thinking.

The accepted aliases are deliberately bounded: bare, dot, hyphen, and provider-prefixed Opus 4.7/4.8 identifiers resolve as adaptive; near-matches and later unknown versions do not silently enter the adaptive path.

51:T115f,Closes #16298.

## Outcome

Cerebras can spend a capped response budget on hidden reasoning and return no visible text. This correction sends `reasoning_effort` only for the two current Cerebras models whose documented contract supports it:

- `gpt-oss-120b`: defaults to `low`; `eliza.thinking="off"` also resolves to `low`, because Cerebras does not accept `none` for this model.
- `zai-glm-4.7`: defaults to `low`; `eliza.thinking="off"` resolves to `none`, so planner/formatting calls consume zero hidden reasoning tokens.

Provider-prefixed forms (`cerebras:`, `cerebras/`, and `openai/`) normalize to the exact bare id. Family substrings and undocumented lookalikes—including generic GLM/Gemma names, `gpt-oss-20b`, and `zai-glm-4.6`—receive no inferred wire field. An explicit valid caller option remains authoritative.

The native result now preserves `usage.reasoningTokens`, which makes the suppression result observable to callers and to the credentialed live receipt. The exact supported model names and suppression values follow the current [Cerebras reasoning documentation](https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/capabilities/reasoning).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Rejects finite gateway timings whose rounded representation overflows to
  `Infinity`, preserving existing `Server-Timing` content instead of emitting an
  invalid metric.
- Keeps the Hono status-101 fallback documentation aligned with the actual
  Workerd extension behavior without changing middleware order or CORS policy.
- Registers the realtime voice settings in the Cloud env example and supplies inert values through the E2E harness.
- Adds a production-faithful raw RFC 6455 E2E through the real voice WebSocket
  route, including handshake headers, middleware headers, error payload, close
  code/reason, and transport closure.
- Sets the accepted Workerd server socket to `binaryType = "arraybuffer"` so a
  binary-first client reaches the intended `hello_required` protocol boundary.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking) plus regression coverage.

# Documentation changes needed?

Yes. The Cloud shared environment example now documents the realtime voice
settings needed by local and deployed Workers; the middleware's inline contract
documentation was also corrected.

55:T14ab,# Relates to

Refs #16223 (route-boundary portion — see scope below)

- [x] Targets `develop`, rebased on latest `origin/develop`, zero conflicts.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change from the evidence below without reading the code.

# Risks

Low. Two route handlers stop reporting an absent notification-service instance as a healthy empty inbox. Behaviour only changes when the service is **not** a live registered instance; the registered happy path is untouched. A runtime that doesn't expose the status getter falls back to the previous empty-inbox behaviour, so partial/headless runtimes are unaffected.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

After #16207 made `NotificationService.start()` fail closed on an unreadable durable inbox, `AgentRuntime._runServiceStart()` marks the service `failed` and leaves **no instance**. Both notification routes returned HTTP 200 `{notifications:[],unreadCount:0}` whenever `runtime.getService(NOTIFICATION)` was absent — so they converted that failure back into a *healthy empty inbox*, masking lost/at-risk persistence. The HTTP comment even claimed the UI "degrades gracefully and retries" when it did not.

This consults the runtime's existing `getServiceRegistrationStatus` (read defensively — it lives on the concrete `AgentRuntime`, not `IAgentRuntime`, mirroring the `runtimeLike` pattern already used by plugin route handlers) instead of inferring health from a missing instance:

- `failed` → typed 503 `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_FAILED` (no adapter/secret detail), never an empty success;
- `pending` / `registering` / registered-without-instance → 503 + `Retry-After` so the client backs off instead of caching an empty history;
- `unknown` (native feature disabled / headless) → the explicit no-inbox contract: 200 empty on GET, positively identified from status.

Applied identically to the HTTP route (`packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.ts`) and the Android UDS route (`plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.ts`).

## Scope

This is the **route-boundary** portion of #16223 (items 1–5, 8, 9). The UI hydrate-retry (item 6) and single-flight runtime recovery (item 7) are larger and left as explicit follow-ups rather than half-done here — the issue stays open (`Refs`, not `Closes`).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking; data-integrity / fail-closed correctness).

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The `if (!service)` branch in both route files. Pinned by the new status-driven cases in `notification-routes.test.ts` and `dispatch.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

None — route-level unit tests cover the matrix.
