# Risks

Low. Desktop action behavior is unchanged. Capability metadata now reports the resolved nutjs or legacy driver used by the service instead of unrelated legacy binaries. The main risk is test-lane classification: a future filename, config, or manifest change could accidentally make real desktop actuation discoverable by ordinary sweeps. The manifest contract test and unconditional `*.real.test.ts` package exclusion guard that boundary.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Keeps real `AgentRuntime` + `InMemoryDatabaseAdapter` integration coverage in the deterministic service suite while removing screenshot capture, valid pointer/keyboard/scroll payloads, and window enumeration from ordinary runs.
- Moves observable real screenshot, cursor-movement readback, and window checks into `service.real.test.ts`, guarded by per-command `COMPUTER_USE_REAL_DESKTOP_TESTS=1` acknowledgment and executed through `ComputerUseService`, the selected driver seam.
- Makes permission, driver, screenshot-quality, and empty-window failures fail the real lane instead of silently skipping or fabricating healthy results.
- Uses the public `@elizaos/core` export for the shared runtime harness instead of a cross-workspace source import.
- Reports screenshot and input capability through the service's resolved driver, with deterministic Linux coverage for nutjs hosts without xdotool.
- Registers and documents the destructive real lane and adds a manifest contract ensuring the deterministic suite has no direct host-module imports.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and test-integrity improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated in `plugins/plugin-computeruse/docs/TEST_LANES_COMPUTERUSE_VISION.md` with the explicit isolated-desktop command and its host-side effects.

## Known gaps / failures

- The destructive `COMPUTER_USE_REAL_DESKTOP_TESTS=1` lane was **not run** on this shared development host. It captures the display and moves/clicks the pointer, sends keys, scrolls, and enumerates windows, so it requires an isolated interactive desktop with explicit operator consent.
- The same real file was invoked without acknowledgment and failed before test collection. This proves the fail-closed safety gate, not real hardware behavior.
- Root lint reported existing warning-level diagnostics across the monorepo; `bun run verify` exited successfully with no errors.57:Te26,## Summary

Fixes #16172 — three stacked gaps on the plain-text connector command path that let a **leading bot-mention bypass the deterministic command layer**, after which the planner improvised (hallucinated success) and could reach the **ungated MODEL_SWITCH action**.

On strict-mention Discord servers a command arrives as `<@bot> /model show` with the mention still in `content.text`. The deterministic layer (`hasCommand`/`detectCommand` and the pre-LLM explicit shortcut gate) only matched text starting with `/`, so the mention-prefixed command fell through to the planner.

## The three gaps (issue framing) → fixes

**Gap 1 — mention prefix defeats detection.** Strip a leading connector-native mention (`<@id>` / `<@!id>`) before command detection:
- `plugins/plugin-commands` parser: new `stripLeadingBotMention()`, applied in `hasCommand`, `detectCommand`, and `normalizeCommandBody`.
- core explicit-shortcut matcher: new `stripLeadingMentionForShortcut()`, applied at the top of `matchShortcut` (explicit tier only; the natural tier already dissolves mentions via `normalizeForMatch`).
- Only a **single leading** mention is stripped — an inner mention like `/model <@123>` is untouched.

**Gap 2 — planner improvises / hallucinated success.** With detection restored, a mention-prefixed command now resolves deterministically **before** the planner, closing the fabricated-"Switched…"-success path (same class as #7923/#7945).

**Gap 3 — MODEL_SWITCH has no role gate.** `MODEL_SWITCH` now declares `roleGate: { minRole: "OWNER" }` (was `USER`). Flipping local↔cloud is a **global** runtime inference switch that drives the same loopback `/api/runtime/model-switch` route the owner-only `/model local|cloud` slash write uses — and matches the sibling `AGENT_SWITCH` gate. Enforced by the shared `actionGateFailure` chokepoint that both the shortcut gate and the planned-tool-call executor consult, so a planner-routed non-owner message can no longer invoke it.

- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/parser.ts` — mention-strip in detection path
- `packages/core/src/runtime/shortcut-registry.ts` — mention-strip in explicit shortcut matcher
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/model-switch.ts` — OWNER gate

## Tests (new / updated)

- **parser** (`plugin-commands/__tests__/parser.test.ts`): mention-prefixed detection — plain, `<@!id>` nickname, newline separator; arg preservation; non-command mention; inner-mention safety; `normalizeCommandBody` raw-mention forms.
- **shortcut-registry** (`packages/core/.../shortcut-registry.test.ts`): explicit alias behind a leading mention (incl. `allowNatural:false`), inner-mention safety, `stripLeadingMentionForShortcut`.
- **model-switch** (`plugin-app-control/.../model-switch.test.ts`): OWNER-gate regression via `satisfiesRoleGate` — USER/MEMBER/ADMIN rejected, OWNER allowed; declared-gate assertion updated USER→OWNER.

5a:T1e79,Closes #16185

## Problem

`.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml` verified the served `/api/health` commit against `inputs.expected_gateway_sha` before and after the paired benchmark, but never asserted that the workflow checkout itself (`GITHUB_SHA`) was that same commit. If `develop` advanced between an exact staging deployment and workflow dispatch, the artifact could combine scripts/config from a newer checkout with a gateway deployment at the requested older SHA — and every served-SHA check would still pass.

## Change

- **New fail-closed bind step** (`Bind checkout to the requested gateway SHA`) in the `live` job, placed immediately after Node setup — before the health check and before any paid request. It re-validates `/^[a-f0-9]{40}$/` on `expected_gateway_sha` and requires `GITHUB_SHA === expected_gateway_sha`, with a clear remediation message on mismatch. The step lives only in the dispatch-gated `live` job (`if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`); the `pull_request` contract job is untouched.
- **Both provenance SHAs in the privacy-safe summary**: the timing-table step now prints `Checkout: $GITHUB_SHA` alongside the gateway deployment SHA (labeled as requested + health-verified before/after, since the summary runs on `always()` and must not overclaim if the reverify step failed). (The JSONL artifact already stamps both per record via `ciContext()` in `packages/scripts/cloud/chat-latency.mjs` — `ci.sha` and `ci.gatewayDeploySha` — no script change needed.)
- **Documented dispatch sequence** in the workflow header: deploy exact `develop` commit → confirm `refs/heads/develop` still equals the served SHA → dispatch on `develop` with that SHA; explicitly states there is no feature-branch or stale-checkout escape hatch (canonical-ref guards preserved, no deploy bypass added).
- **Contract test extended** (`packages/scripts/__tests__/chat-latency-workflow.test.ts`): executes the embedded bind program exactly as the runner does (`node --input-type=module -` with the extracted heredoc on stdin, fully controlled env) for match / mismatch / missing-`GITHUB_SHA` / malformed-SHA cases, proves the bind step is ordered inside the `live` job before the health check and the benchmark command, asserts the summary emits both SHAs, and adds the bind step to the shell + embedded-Node syntax validation sweep.

## Acceptance criteria mapping

- Fail before any paid request unless `github.sha == inputs.expected_gateway_sha` → bind step, ordered before `Verify the exact deployed gateway SHA` and the probe; ordering statically asserted in the contract test.
- Keep the 40-char validation and before/after served-health checks → bind step re-validates the 40-char shape; `Verify`/`Reverify the exact deployed gateway SHA` steps unchanged (existing tests still assert them).
- Emit both checkout and served SHAs in the privacy-safe summary/artifact → summary prints both; artifact records already stamp `ci.sha` + `ci.gatewayDeploySha`.
- Static workflow contract proving a mismatch fails before the benchmark → `fails on a checkout/deployment mismatch before any benchmark command` + ordering test.
- Preserve canonical-ref guards; no feature-branch deploy bypass → no guard touched; header comment states the invariant and forbids bypass.
- Document the exact merged-`develop` dispatch sequence → workflow header comment block; presence asserted by test.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync.

# Risks

Low. On restart, resumable SHA-pinned downloads now hash an existing `.part` once before making a network request. Incomplete or mismatched partials continue through the existing Range-resume path unchanged. The tradeoff is one local read of an existing partial after restart.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

If the process stopped after writing the final byte but before renaming the staged file, the next run sent `Range: bytes=<file-size>-`. A conforming server answered `416 Range Not Satisfiable`, so a fully downloaded multi-GB model remained stuck in `.part` forever.

This change verifies a resumed `.part` against the manifest SHA-256 before opening the network request. When it already matches, the downloader atomically promotes it to the final path and removes the stale `.expected` sidecar. Non-matching partials retain the existing resume behavior.

The regression test uses a real local TCP HTTP server with `206`/`416` range semantics and proves that a complete staged text artifact is committed without any request for that artifact.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

5c:Tbb8,Fixes #16162.

## Why

`ai-pricing/lookup.ts` resolves the input and output rates concurrently (#16156). On a fresh isolate both sides can miss the completed-value cache and reach the same canonical provider/model/rate read before either result is cached — **two identical external-catalog / DB fetches for one key**. `cache.ts` kept the completed-value TTL cache but retained **no in-flight promise**, so nothing coalesced the concurrent cold misses.

Not a billing-correctness bug (prices still fail closed + are validated) — a duplicate-I/O / cold-latency opportunity, and the persisted read runs on **every** inference in the synchronous credit-reserve.

## What

A small key-scoped single-flight (`coalesce`) shared by both cache layers (external catalog + persisted DB):

- concurrent cold misses for the same key await **one** in-flight loader;
- the in-flight promise is dropped on **both fulfillment and rejection**, so a failed load never poisons a later retry and the map is bounded by completion (attached as a settled `then(cleanup, cleanup)` handler so it never spawns a second unhandled rejected chain);
- the completed-value TTL cache, external-404 negative-cache, DB-errors-not-cached, and fail-closed validation are all **unchanged** (only cold misses reach the coalescer; a cache hit still returns immediately).

## Testing

`bun test ai-pricing/cache.test.ts` — **7 pass**, incl. two new regressions: two concurrent cold misses for the same key invoke the loader **once** (persisted + external). The existing negative-cache / DB-error-retry / cross-key-independence tests still hold (the sequential retry test already covers drop-on-reject: a second call after a failure re-runs the loader).

`typecheck` clean, biome clean.

Closes the `/v1/messages` gap in the #16081 reservation-parity invariant series (fixed for `/v1/chat/completions` in #16099, and the A2A / MCP agent routes in #16147 / #16148). Found while auditing cloud call sites that reserve credits and then call the provider.

- [x] Targets `develop`, rebased on latest `origin/develop`, zero conflicts.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change from the evidence below without reading the code.

# Risks

Low. Reasoning-model requests now reserve the (larger) ceiling the provider is already capped at; short completions release the difference through the existing reconciliation. Non-reasoning models are unaffected (`messagesEffectiveMaxTokens` passes their budget through unchanged). No reservation/reconciliation reordering.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`messagesEffectiveMaxTokens` raises a reasoning model's provider `maxOutputTokens` above the requested `max_tokens` — a requested `256` becomes the `4096` reasoning floor, and an Anthropic CoT budget adds thinking + a response floor — so hidden reasoning doesn't consume the whole budget and return empty output (the fix for the `gpt-oss-120b` / `zai-glm-4.7` empty-output failure).

But the reservation was still priced on the raw `request.max_tokens`. So a reasoning request admitted far fewer output tokens than the provider was authorized to bill (256 reserved vs 4096 authorized — a 16× gap), letting the provider outrun the reservation and surface an uncollected overage. That's exactly the #16081 invariant — reservation admission must equal the provider-facing budget — which was fixed for `/v1/chat/completions` and the agent routes but not `/v1/messages`.

The fix computes the effective (floored) ceiling once, before the reservation, and prices against it. Both provider paths (`generateText` / `streamText`) already recompute the identical deterministic value, so admission and enforcement now match.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking; billing correctness).

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cloud/api/v1/messages/route.ts` — `estimatedOutputTokens` is now `messagesEffectiveMaxTokens(...)` instead of the raw `request.max_tokens`. Pinned by the new case in `messages-billing-offpath.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

None — the route-level regression test covers it.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)64:T5b6,Bots cannot invoke Discord application commands, so until now the native picker path (sender auth → connector gate → deterministic resolve or pipeline dispatch) could only be exercised by a human clicking every command — which is how the `/model show` verification happened, and why command rot ("stub reading settings that never existed") kept slipping through.

This sweep **simulates the native invocation for every catalog command on the discord surface**, twice each:

New commands added to the catalog get swept automatically with zero test-writing.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)65:Tdf2,## What

Both pool codex accounts hit "Your access token could not be refreshed because your refresh token was revoked" on 2026-07-13 — breaking the multi-account pool's core promise (store credentials durably, never re-login). Root cause: OpenAI refresh tokens are ONE-TIME-USE, and a rotated pair could evaporate two ways:

1. **Credentials store default**: codex's `cli_auth_credentials_store` defaults to `auto`, which may pick a keyring/ephemeral store — on a headless box the rotated pair then never reaches `CODEX_HOME/auth.json`, the only place `adoptRotatedCodexTokens` (#11033) can harvest it. Forensics: both pool records had `updatedAt == createdAt` (no rotation ever harvested) while sessions had run and refreshed.
2. **Materialize clobber**: `materializeCodexHome` unconditionally overwrote `auth.json` with pool tokens and stamped `last_refresh = now` — a concurrent/just-exited session's rotated pair could be replaced by the already-consumed pool copy (and the forged timestamp defeats adoption's newer-only gate).

Either way the next canonical refresh presents a consumed token → OpenAI reuse-detection revokes the whole grant family → `needs-reauth` on a perfectly-managed account.

### Fixes
- Pin `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` in the materialized `config.toml` so rotations always land where adoption can harvest them. Value validated against the pinned codex-acp parser (enum `file|keyring|auto|ephemeral`; an unknown variant fails the WHOLE config parse — the same trap as PINNED_CODEX_ACP_EFFORTS).
- `materializeCodexHome` now adopts rotated tokens (mutex-serialized, newer-only) immediately before writing, and pairs the written `access_token` from the freshly-reloaded pool record so a consumed generation is never re-paired with an adopted refresh token.
- `markNeedsReauth` logs the transition loudly with the failing cause — next time this fires, one grep names the culprit instead of a forensic dig.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)66:Tb87,## What

When `ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI` routes the chat brain through plugin-cli-inference (the TOS-clean Claude Max / Codex SDK lane), asking the agent "what model are you running on?" reported the **cerebras triage model** (e.g. `gemma-4-31b`) instead of the Claude/Codex model actually serving the reply.

Root cause: the plugin registers plain handler functions via the declarative `models:` map with no per-slot metadata. The `RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT` provider resolves a slot's model by preferring the **winning** provider's declared `metadata.displayModel`; with none present it falls through to the generic `OLLAMA_/OPENAI_/ANTHROPIC_/"" × SMALL_MODEL` prefix walk — which names a *losing* provider's configured model for a slot the priority-100 cli-inference plugin actually owns.

Fix: add `modelMetadata` to the plugin, resolving each served slot's `displayModel` from the **same env precedence** as `resolveSdkModel`/`resolveCodexModel` (large tiers → `ELIZA_CLI_{CLAUDE,CODEX}_MODEL`; `ACTION_PLANNER` → `ELIZA_CLI_{CLAUDE,CODEX}_PLANNER_MODEL` with large-tier fallback), gated to the active backend and to text-planner mode. The runtime already forwards `plugin.modelMetadata?.[modelType]` into `registerModel` (runtime.ts:2077), so the self-report now names the concrete model.

## Evidence

68:T1950,## What & Why

Two REAL user-observed bugs on the Eliza Cloud OAuth loading/landing flow, seen by Shadow on staging (`app-staging.elizacloud.ai` / `staging.elizacloud.ai`) on 2026-07-11. Both fixed at root cause. **Navigation + asset only — no auth semantics changed.**

---

### BUG 1 — post-provision auto-redirect never fires (user stuck on the loading/provisioning screen)

**Root cause.** The post-OAuth `/join` provisioning landing (`JoinPage`) runs the join flow and then hard-navigates into the app:

```ts
// packages/ui/src/cloud/join/JoinPage.tsx
const result = await runJoinFlow({ ... });
setPhase("ready");
if (typeof window !== "undefined") {
  window.location.assign("/");
}
```

That only fires once `useJoinSessionAuth()` reports `authenticated`, which needs the Steward runtime to resolve the session. Its own registration says so:

```ts
// packages/ui/src/cloud/join/register.ts
// "it needs the Steward runtime so the session resolves"
```

But `/join` was **missing from `STEWARD_RUNTIME_ROUTE_PATTERNS`**:

```ts
// packages/ui/src/cloud/shell/StewardProvider.tsx  (before)
const STEWARD_RUNTIME_ROUTE_PATTERNS = [
  /^\/app-auth(?:\/|$)/, /^\/auth(?:\/|$)/, /^\/bsc(?:\/|$)/,
  /^\/dashboard(?:\/|$)/, /^\/login(?:\/|$)/, /^\/invite(?:\/|$)/,
  /^\/accept-invitation(?:\/|$)/, /^\/payment(?:\/|$)/,
  /^\/sensitive-requests(?:\/|$)/, /^\/approve(?:\/|$)/, /^\/ballot(?:\/|$)/,
] as const; // no /join

function shouldLoadStewardRuntime(pathname: string): boolean {
  if (readStoredToken()) return true;               // token-present short-circuit
  return STEWARD_RUNTIME_ROUTE_PATTERNS.some((p) => p.test(pathname));
}
```

So the runtime only mounted for `/join` when a token was **already** in `localStorage`. On the returning-user / cookie-session handback from OAuth the token isn't stored yet → `LocalStewardAuthContext` stays `null` → `useJoinSessionAuth` never becomes `authenticated` → `JoinPage` sits on the provisioning spinner (or `<Navigate to="/login?returnTo=/join">`) and the `window.location.assign("/")` never runs.

**Fix.** Add `/join` to the runtime patterns (matching the register.ts contract), and export `shouldLoadStewardRuntime` for coverage. File anchor: `packages/ui/src/cloud/shell/StewardProvider.tsx:58-79`.

---

### BUG 2 — Eliza logo renders broken on the OAuth loading screen

**Root cause.** The preboot shell logo used a hard **relative** path:

```html
<!-- packages/app/index.html:378 (before) -->
<img class="eliza-preboot-shell__mark" src="./brand/logos/logo_white_nobg.svg" alt="" />
```

The deployed web SPA (`ELIZA_WEB_ABSOLUTE_BASE=1` → base `"/"`) serves nested routes from the origin root, so the browser resolves `./brand/...` against the **route directory**, not root. On a deep OAuth route that becomes `/auth/brand/...` or `/app-auth/brand/...`, which the Pages SPA catch-all serves as `index.html`.

**Evidence (staging curls):**

```
/auth/brand/logos/logo_white_nobg.svg      → HTTP 200  text/html      ← wrong (SPA fallback)
/app-auth/brand/logos/logo_white_nobg.svg  → HTTP 200  text/html      ← wrong
/brand/logos/logo_white_nobg.svg           → HTTP 200  image/svg+xml  ← correct SVG
```

The `<img>` received HTML instead of an SVG → broken-image icon.

**Fix.** Use Vite's base-aware `%BASE_URL%` token (Vite's `htmlEnvHook` replaces `%ENV%` in `index.html`; `BASE_URL` = resolved `base`):

```html
<!-- packages/app/index.html:378 (after) -->
<img class="eliza-preboot-shell__mark" src="%BASE_URL%brand/logos/logo_white_nobg.svg" alt="" />
```

- Web build (`base: "/"`) → `/brand/logos/...` → resolves at every route depth.
- Native/desktop build (`base: "./"`, Electrobun/Capacitor load relative to the HTML doc) → `./brand/logos/...` → resolves the bundled asset.

A hard `/brand/...` would regress native (resolves outside the bundle); a hard `./brand/...` regressed deep web routes. The token satisfies both. (Addresses the codex `--uncommitted` P2 base-awareness note.)

---

## Tests

- **`packages/ui/src/cloud/shell/StewardProvider.test.tsx`** — new: `/join` loads the runtime with no stored token; `shouldLoadStewardRuntime` unit coverage (join + subpaths, existing auth routes, non-auth routes incl. `/rejoinder` substring guard, token-present short-circuit). **9/9 pass.**
- **`packages/app/test/brand-surface.test.ts`** — new: preboot logo uses base-aware `%BASE_URL%` and must not regress to a hard relative `./brand` path. **12/12 pass.**
- Existing `run-join-flow.test.ts` still green (8/8).

```

✓ test/brand-surface.test.ts (12 tests)
✓ src/cloud/join/lib/run-join-flow.test.ts (8 tests)
```

Biome clean on all 4 changed files; tsgo shows no new errors in the changed files (pre-existing repo errors are in `core/src/cloud-routing.ts` and `api/client-cloud*`, untouched here).

## Scope

`packages/app/index.html`, `packages/ui/src/cloud/shell/StewardProvider.tsx` + 2 test files. No auth semantics touched — this is navigation gating + a base-aware asset path.

# Risks

`generated-registry.json`. No runtime, core, or UI code is touched. Worst
case is a malformed entry, which `bun run --cwd packages/registry validate`
guards against (run clean).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds `plugin-wallettriage` to the third-party plugin registry.

WalletTriage is a real-time wallet risk engine: the plugin ships a
`CHECK_ADDRESS_RISK` action that, given an EVM address in the message,
cross-references the address's active ERC20 approvals with a live exploit
threat feed and returns a risk score (0–100), risk level and worst-first
findings (what to revoke, USD at risk) — so an agent can triage a
counterparty (or its own wallet) BEFORE acting. Each query is paid via
x402 (~$0.01 USDC on Base) by a configurable low-balance session key;
stateless, no signup, no API key. Hardening: https enforced for remote
gateways, per-query payment cap (refuses inflated payment requirements),
strict address validation.

- npm: https://www.npmjs.com/package/plugin-wallettriage
- Code: https://github.com/wallettriage/plugin-wallettriage
- Service: https://wallettriage.com · OpenAPI: https://api.wallettriage.com/openapi.yaml

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking): adds one community registry entry (data only).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/registry/entries/third-party/plugin-wallettriage.json` — then
confirm `generated-registry.json` contains the derived entry and that
validation passes.

# WALLETTRIAGE_PRIVATE_KEY=0x... (session wallet with USDC on Base)
# ask the agent: "Is 0xd8dA6BF26964aF9D7eEd9e03E53415D37aA96045 safe to interact with?"
```

<!--
## Discord username
<seu_username> (se estiver no Discord ai16z)
-->6c:T5e6,Found by battery-testing `/reset` through the GUI chat route: it replied **"Reset this room: cleared command settings and 31126 message(s)"** for a ~40-message scratch room. The delete was correctly room-scoped (global count 31,126 → 31,077 — only the room's ~49 rows went); **the count lied**.

Root cause is interface/implementation drift: `IAgentRuntime`/adapter declare `countMemories({ roomIds?: UUID[] … })` and every typed caller uses that spelling (plugin-commands' `/reset`/`/new` report, the compact action's boundary math) — but the runtime implementation only read the legacy `roomId` singular and **silently dropped `roomIds`**, so interface-correct room-scoped counts came back table-wide. TypeScript blessed the callers because they matched the interface; the implementation was the bug. Same silent-drop family as the `getMemories`-ignored-`limit` keystone from the prod-readiness audit.

Fix: forward `roomIds` through (keeping `roomId` and the bare-UUID form working), and pin all four scoping shapes with a recording-adapter contract test.

Live-verified after fix (see below in thread): `/reset` on a fresh scratch room reports the true small count.

Tests: new `runtime-count-memories.test.ts` 4✓; core typecheck ✓; plugin-commands typecheck ✓; biome clean. Downstream side-effect: the compact action's `completeMessageWindow` math is now computed against the actual room instead of the entire agent history.

**Drop `/models`.** It had shrunk to a one-line duplicate of `/model show` ("Current model: X"). Removed from the registry, the deterministic keys, and the coverage matrix — pickers everywhere drop it on the next registration push (live-verified: Discord now serves 29 commands, `/models` gone).

**Name the live endpoint in `/model show`.** The `OPENAI_*` family can point anywhere OpenAI-compatible (Cerebras, Eliza Cloud, …) via `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, so the model id alone doesn't tell the operator who's actually answering. The chat lines now carry it, live output:

```
Model configuration:
small: gemma-4-31b — effort low (app config, via api.cerebras.ai)
large: zai-glm-4.7 — effort low (app config, via api.cerebras.ai)
coding — default backend: eliza (app config)
  codex: gpt-5.6-terra @ xhigh (app config)
  claude: claude-opus-4-8 @ high (app config)
  ...
```

Hosts resolve from `OPENAI_BASE_URL` / `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` at show time (unparsable URL degrades to no suffix rather than a broken line).

The prior test bypassed lazy service registration, runtime lifecycle, cache persistence, and real service lookup. It validated a synthetic registry contract rather than the internal architecture used in production.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The `develop` push for #16297 exposed a pre-existing Biome failure in the plugin-elizacloud reasoning-effort test. A read-only repository-wide formatter audit found exactly three sibling failures in plugin-commands and core. This PR applies the canonical formatter output to those four files so the `Format + Type Safety Ratchet` lane can pass without carrying unrelated reflows.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking repository formatting repair).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This changes only source layout and test layout; public behavior and documentation are unchanged.

# Testing

bun run verify
git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD
```

## Summary

- remove the Autodocs workflow whose `packages/autodoc` implementation is absent
- remove the two historical `packages/computeruse` release workflows while preserving the active `@elizaos/plugin-computeruse` package
- remove the Feed migration workflow whose only push triggers target absent `staging` and `production` branches
- document the reachable authorities: `release.yaml` for the active computer-use plugin and Railway's `railway.json` -> `railway-start.sh` boundary for Feed schema provisioning, where schema push is explicitly best-effort and startup continues if it fails

## Root cause and impact

These entry points survived after their source packages or trigger branches disappeared. Two release workflows still woke up for every GitHub Release only to skip every job, Autodocs could not generate documentation without its package, and the Feed workflow had no reachable branch trigger. Removing them makes the Actions catalog describe executable product paths and prevents release events from creating misleading skipped workflows.

The change does not alter the active ComputerUse plugin, Feed runtime, schema, application code, or any rendered UI.

# Risks

Low. The checkout is exclusively managed by the provisioning service. Recursively normalizing ownership adds a short pre-deploy filesystem pass but prevents root-owned metadata or tracked files from blocking immutable checkout.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Normalizes ownership for the complete `/opt/eliza` managed checkout before the workflow runs any mutating Git command.

## Why?

Production run `29220180000` failed at `git reset --hard HEAD`: prior root activity had left tracked source files root-owned. The existing repair covered only `.git`, so Git running as `deploy` could not unlink or replace those files. After applying `chown -R deploy:deploy /opt/eliza` on the host, attempt 2 completed successfully at `249403b5364d40a8e1db37e2a6b2bbb2e39fee14`.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The workflow comment documents the ownership contract next to the command.

*   **[elizaos/eliza#13681](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/13681):** The desktop-packaged E2E lane remains red and requires three consecutive green runs to be considered stable.

## ✅ Completed Work

### Cloud Infrastructure & Authentication
*   Implemented atomic, single-use CLI key retrieval and idempotent session completion to eliminate race conditions ([elizaos/eliza#16190](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16190), [#16257](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16257), [#16259](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16259)).
*   Hardened WebSocket telemetry boundaries and unified MCP chat reserved ceilings ([elizaos/eliza#16098](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16098), [#16301](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16301), [#16234](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16234)).

### Orchestrator & Agent Reliability
*   Improved provisioning safety for `eliza-code` and implemented deterministic gating for sub-agent completion ([elizaos/eliza#16196](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16196), [#16255](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16255)).
*   Consolidated account provider management and ensured durable refresh-token rotation ([elizaos/eliza#16204](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16204), [#16293](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16293)).

### Testing & Build Infrastructure
*   Enhanced test realism by migrating suites from mocks to real `AgentRuntime` instances ([elizaos/eliza#16248](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16248), [#16250](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16250), [#16252](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16252)).
*   Consolidated native build workflows for Android and iOS to ensure consistent FFI library production ([elizaos/eliza#16287](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16287)).

- Project board: N/A - post-merge repository-integrity correction is not assigned to a product board.
- Parent defect: PR #16271, merge `ab1f1bd870cc8d69f3b44c979c138ae0ec84dbff`.
- Original authored evidence: issue #9299 and PR #9543.
- Suggested lane tag: codex-authored-report-retention

## Problem

PR #16271 deleted and ignored records that its exact-head review had identified as authored, durable evidence rather than disposable generated residue:

- `plugins/plugin-training/docs/audit/9299-gepa-live/RESULTS.md` plus three optimized-prompt JSON artifacts and three real-model run logs. PR #9543 and issue #9299 explicitly identify these as the human-verifiable live GEPA evidence and real-model trajectories for the completed Scope 5 claim.
- Two authored Ralphy Markdown benchmark reports under the misspelled `benchmark_resukts` output tree.

The native evidence subset was retained before merge, but these records were not.

## Scope

Restore only the durable authored records. Keep disposable run-output directories ignored. Move the Ralphy prose into its maintained benchmark documentation tree instead of weakening the generated-output ignore. Add an executable repository contract that distinguishes retained records from disposable output.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The complete seven-file GEPA audit archive from PR #9543 is restored byte-for-byte and remains reachable from its authored summary.
- [ ] The two authored Ralphy reports are retained in the maintained `ralphy/` documentation tree and linked from its README.
- [ ] New/unlisted GEPA audit output and the misspelled OpenClaw results tree remain ignored.
- [ ] A focused contract test proves the immutable archive hashes, documentation links, and ignore behavior.
- [ ] Focused checks and repository verification pass after syncing with `origin/develop`.

## Evidence Plan

- Screenshots/video: N/A - documentation and repository-integrity correction; no rendered UI source changes.
- Backend/frontend logs: N/A - no runtime path changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: the restored GEPA `.run.log` records are the original gpt-oss-120b trajectories introduced and reviewed in PR #9543; no new model behavior is being claimed.
- Domain artifacts: exact SHA-256 inventory of the restored GEPA archive and migrated Ralphy reports, plus `git check-ignore` contract output.
85:T1350,## Problem

#16207 intentionally changed `NotificationService.start()` to fail when persisted notification state cannot be read instead of treating an unreadable durable inbox as a healthy empty inbox (the acceptance criterion in #16206).

That fail-closed contract is currently lost at the next boundary:

- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.ts` returns HTTP 200 with `{ notifications: [], unreadCount: 0 }` for `GET /api/notifications` whenever `runtime.getService(ServiceType.NOTIFICATION)` is absent.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.ts` has the same fallback for the Android UDS route.
- Neither route distinguishes an intentionally disabled/unregistered service from `pending`, `registering`, or `failed` service state. After a cache-read failure, `AgentRuntime._runServiceStart()` marks the service `failed` and leaves no instance, so both routes convert the failure back into a healthy empty inbox.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.ts` hydrates once. On a transport/5xx failure it logs, sets `hydrated: true`, preserves the live WebSocket subscription, but schedules no HTTP hydration retry. A transient startup race or recovered persistence layer can therefore leave persisted history absent for the whole UI session.
- The HTTP route comment says the UI “degrades gracefully and retries,” but no such retry currently exists.

This is a fail-closed integration/recovery gap. It is **not** the packaged App Live native-notification failure: that test injects a mock WebSocket `agent_event`, does not start core `NotificationService`, and the observed failing run predates #16207.

## Required behavior

1. Preserve #16207's fail-closed persistence semantics. Do not restore the blanket `hydrate()` catch that turns an unreadable inbox into empty state.
2. Give notification routes enough runtime lifecycle information to distinguish:
   - intentionally disabled/unregistered notification support;
   - `pending` / `registering` startup;
   - `failed` startup;
   - a registered service instance.
3. For `pending` / `registering`, return a typed retryable 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_NOT_READY`) and a bounded `Retry-After` value instead of HTTP 200 empty.
4. For `failed`, return a typed 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_FAILED`) without leaking adapter/secrets/internal error details. Never represent failed persistence as an empty successful inbox.
5. Define the intentional disabled/headless contract explicitly. If that case remains HTTP 200 empty, it must be positively identified rather than inferred from a missing service instance.
6. Add bounded, jittered/background UI hydrate retry that:
   - honors `Retry-After` when present;
   - does not clear already received notifications;
   - keeps the live WebSocket subscription active;
   - avoids duplicate subscriptions/delivery;
   - stops after a documented cap and exposes/logs the terminal hydrate error rather than silently reading as healthy empty.
7. Add explicit, single-flight service recovery/backoff for transient notification hydration failures. Recovery may live at the runtime service boundary or in a dedicated route/service coordinator, but requests must not create a retry stampede. A recovered cache adapter must be able to rehydrate persisted history without restarting the entire agent.
8. Apply equivalent semantics to the Android UDS notification route.
9. Emit enough structured diagnostics/telemetry to distinguish disabled, not-ready, failed, retrying, recovered, and terminal-retry-exhausted states.

## Tests

- `packages/core/src/services/notification.test.ts`
  - unreadable cache still fails startup;
  - a transient cache-read failure can recover through the chosen explicit recovery path;
  - concurrent recovery requests are single-flight / bounded.
- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.test.ts`
  - pending/registering GET is typed 503 + `Retry-After`, not 200 empty;
  - failed GET is typed 503, not 200 empty;
  - intentionally disabled/unregistered behavior is explicit;
  - registered service returns persisted rows.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.test.ts` (or the canonical store test file)
  - bounded retry and `Retry-After` handling with fake timers;
  - state received over WS is preserved while HTTP hydrate retries;
  - successful recovery merges/sets persisted history without duplicate delivery;
  - terminal exhaustion is observable and does not create an infinite timer/subscription loop.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.test.ts`
  - lifecycle-state response parity with the HTTP route;
  - recovered service serves persisted rows.

## Non-goals

- Do not conflate this with the separate App Live packaged native-notification test failure.
- Do not mask cache/adapter failures as empty state.
- Do not retry every incoming request independently.

Related: #16206, #16207.
86:T7b9,## Problem

`.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml` accepts `expected_gateway_sha` and verifies that `/api/health` serves that SHA before and after the live paired benchmark. It does not also assert that the workflow checkout's `GITHUB_SHA` equals `expected_gateway_sha`.

If `develop` advances between an exact staging deployment and workflow dispatch, the artifact can combine:

- scripts/config from a newer checkout; and
- a gateway deployment at the requested older SHA.

The served-SHA checks still pass, but the artifact is not intrinsically tied to one source revision. We can close the race externally today, but the workflow should make the invariant self-enforcing.

## Evidence

Read-only audit of the post-merge validation path for #16156 found:

- the canonical deploy guard correctly restricts staging to `develop`;
- Cloud CF Deploy stamps and verifies its exact served SHA;
- Chat Latency Live verifies `expected_gateway_sha` before and after the benchmark;
- no job step compares `github.sha` / `GITHUB_SHA` with `inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.

Historical run 29171752918 is still valid because the run/deploy provenance was checked separately. This issue hardens future artifacts.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Fail before any paid request unless `github.sha == inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.
- [ ] Keep the existing 40-character SHA validation and before/after served-health checks.
- [ ] Emit both checkout and served SHAs in the privacy-safe summary/artifact.
- [ ] Add a self-test or static workflow contract proving a mismatch fails before the benchmark command.
- [ ] Preserve canonical-ref guards; do not add a feature-branch deploy bypass.
- [ ] Document the exact merged-`develop` dispatch sequence.

## Safe interim procedure

Before dispatch, require `refs/heads/develop` to equal the served deployment SHA; immediately after dispatch, require the run's `headSha` to equal the same SHA. Stop without running the benchmark if either check fails.

- [ ] Reconciliation and `auth.json` materialization are one atomic critical section with respect to every in-process selector and external Codex writer sharing that account home; no rotated generation can be overwritten in the adoption/write gap.
- [ ] Rotation freshness uses observable token/file state and does not invent a synthetic `last_refresh` generation that can eclipse a later valid rotation.
- [ ] Writing a configuration that pins `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` is required and fails closed on read/write/replacement failure.
- [ ] Real filesystem concurrency tests exercise a rotating external process, concurrent selectors, adoption failure, and unwritable/config replacement failures without mocks standing in for the shared-file boundary.
- [ ] The branch is rebased on current `origin/develop`; focused tests, app-core typecheck/Biome, error-policy ratchet, and full `bun run verify` pass on the final head.

- Project board: N/A - release integrity work is not assigned to a current product board.
- Parent dependency: #16276 supplies the immutable release plan and fail-closed primitives.
- Coordination Discussion: N/A - scope and acceptance live in this issue.
- Suggested lane tag: codex-transactional-npm

## Scope

Replace the current single-job npm release script with a retry-safe, least-privilege workflow that stages and verifies the complete immutable package cohort before any public channel or GitHub release advances.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The npm workflow requires explicit `source_sha`, exact semver `version`, and `channel`; tag-push and `release:created` publication paths are removed.
- [ ] One global release concurrency group serializes every version/channel mutation.
- [ ] Candidate jobs have read-only contents access, disable persisted Git credentials, and build/test/pack/checksum once without npm credentials.
- [ ] Publish jobs download the verified tarball cohort and never rebuild or mutate source/manifests.
- [ ] Missing exact versions publish under a unique internal candidate dist-tag; matching existing versions resume only after integrity comparison.
- [ ] Every package/version/integrity is verified before every package's public `beta`/`latest` tag is promoted and re-verified.
- [ ] Finalization pushes only the exact planned `refs/tags/vX`; matching tags are idempotent and conflicting tags fail. `--follow-tags`, `-X theirs`, `git add -A`, and direct branch pushes are absent.
- [ ] The GitHub release is published only after full registry verification; all direct `develop`/`main` version-sync logic is removed from this workflow.
- [ ] Structural YAML tests, retry/failure matrices, focused integrations, actionlint, and repository verification pass.

## Evidence Plan

8f:T6ea,## Summary

`lookup.ts` now resolves independent input/output pricing reads concurrently to remove serial billing-path latency (#16156). On a fresh isolate where both sides miss the completed-value cache, both tasks can reach the same provider catalog fetch before either result is cached.

This is not a billing correctness bug: invalid/missing prices still fail closed and the returned rates are validated. It is a low-priority duplicate-I/O/cold-latency opportunity.

## Observed shape

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/lookup.ts` starts the input and output lookups concurrently.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/cache.ts` caches completed values, but does not retain an in-flight promise.
- Two same-key cold misses can therefore issue two identical external catalog reads.

## Suggested implementation

Add key-scoped single-flight caching for in-progress catalog resolutions:

- share one promise for concurrent requests for the same canonical provider/model/rate key;
- remove the promise on both fulfillment and rejection;
- keep the existing completed-value TTL cache and fail-closed validation;
- do not let a failed promise poison later retries;
- bound the in-flight map or prove its lifecycle is bounded by completion.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A deterministic test gates the provider/catalog fetch and proves two concurrent same-key cold lookups make exactly one upstream call.
- [ ] Different keys still resolve concurrently.
- [ ] A rejected in-flight fetch is observed by all waiters and a later call retries normally.
- [ ] Invalid/non-finite rates remain rejected; no fallback can under-bill.
- [ ] Typecheck, focused pricing tests, error-policy ratchet, and changed-file coverage pass.

Related: #16156.
90:Tcf6,**Describe the bug**

A clean agent boot can advertise a settled/healthy deferred boot while startup work has failed. Three related paths are currently observable in a fresh packaged-runtime smoke:

- `AgentRuntime` service startup catches exceptions and returns `null` after logging, so the failure never reaches `runtime.reportError` or structured boot health.
- `@elizaos/plugin-inbox` runs its `inbox_migration` service against a fresh PGlite database before the required schema is ready and fails on `ALTER TABLE`.
- `@elizaos/plugin-scheduling` seeds boot tasks before its scheduled-task runner service is available.

This makes a broken initialization pipeline look healthy and violates the fail-fast/observable-boundary error policy.

**To Reproduce**

1. Build and start the real agent with `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`, configuration, HOME, and credential paths pointed at new temporary directories.
2. Load the default first-party plugin set including inbox and scheduling.
3. Wait for the deferred-boot health state to settle.
4. Inspect structured backend logs and the health response.

The logs report service/migration/seed failures while the health surface reports a settled boot.

**Expected behavior**

- A service startup exception is reported through `runtime.reportError` with service/plugin context and is reflected in structured boot failure state.
- Fresh PGlite initialization orders inbox schema work after its prerequisite schema exists; it completes without a suppressed `ALTER TABLE` failure.
- Scheduling boot seeding runs only after the scheduled-task runner service is registered and ready.
- A real isolated fresh-state boot completes without these errors, and injected service failures remain observably failed.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

- N/A - this is a headless agent boot/runtime correctness issue with no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no rendered UI surface.
- Backend logs: package smoke logs show the three failures; exact isolated before/after logs will be attached to the PR after manual review.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A - no frontend path changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or planner behavior changes.
- Domain artifacts: fresh PGlite schema/service registry state and the boot health payload will be captured in the PR evidence.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Reproduce in an isolated state/config/home without ambient credentials.
- [ ] Remove catch-and-null service startup behavior; typed failures are reported and visible at the runtime boundary.
- [ ] Make inbox fresh-schema initialization deterministic and successful on real PGlite.
- [ ] Make scheduling seed ordering depend on runner readiness structurally, not timing.
- [ ] Add real integration coverage for success and injected-failure paths.
- [ ] Run focused tests, error-policy ratchet, and full `bun run verify` after rebasing onto current `origin/develop`.

This was exposed while hardening real Snap/Flatpak/Debian install-and-boot smoke coverage. The package verifier must not be asked to parse logs to compensate for a runtime health surface that claims success after startup failures.
91:T939,## Problem

`zai-glm-4.7` — the default `TEXT_LARGE` model on Cerebras since #16261 — is a hybrid reasoning model (streams a separate `reasoning` delta channel), but `isReasoningModel()` in `plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts` does not match it. Two consequences on a local Cerebras runtime (`ELIZA_PROVIDER=cerebras`, `OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL=zai-glm-4.7`):

1. The Cerebras-mode `reasoning_effort: "low"` default (added for exactly this failure on `gpt-oss-120b`) never applies to GLM, so its reasoning runs unbounded.
2. `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"` — set by core's planner loop (`packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts:1549`), Stage-1 formatting calls (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:8770`), and the app-model-tester — is silently ignored by plugin-openai, while plugin-elizacloud maps it to each Cerebras model's suppression value (`resolveCerebrasThinkingOffReasoningEffort`, `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts:366`).

With a capped `max_tokens` the model spends the entire budget on hidden reasoning and returns **empty visible content** with `finish_reason: "length"`.

## Reproduction (live Cerebras, wire-captured via a logging proxy)

`POST /api/model-tester/run {"test":"text-large"}` on a default local Cerebras setup, 8 runs. Every request went out as `{"model":"zai-glm-4.7","max_tokens":160}` with **no** `reasoning_effort`:

```
4x  finish_reason:"length"  reasoning_tokens:160/160  → "TEXT_LARGE returned empty text"
4x  finish_reason:"stop"    reasoning_tokens:62–157   → "pong"
```

~50% failure, purely dependent on how long GLM happens to reason. Direct curl confirms the mechanism: `reasoning_effort:"low"` bounds reasoning (~20–30 tokens), and `reasoning_effort:"none"` (accepted by gemma/GLM, rejected by gpt-oss) eliminates it entirely (`reasoning_tokens: 0`).

## Fix

PR incoming, mirroring plugin-elizacloud's settled semantics:

- add the GLM family to `isReasoningModel()` so the Cerebras `"low"` default applies;
- honor `eliza.thinking === "off"` in Cerebras mode by mapping it per model: `"none"` for gemma/GLM, `"low"` for gpt-oss; explicit caller `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` still wins.

After the fix (same wire capture): 10/10 probes return content, `finish_reason:"stop"`, `reasoning_tokens: 0`.

9d:T592,Found while battery-testing every slash command through the GUI chat route (`POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/stream`).

**Repro:** in a conversation with enough history, send `/compact`. The deterministic command layer fires correctly (`thought: "Shortcut: cmd:compact"`), the COMPACT_CONVERSATION action succeeds, and its final text is the designed clean summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M."). But the **visible streamed reply** is the compactor's *internal* model call output — the raw rendered-ledger JSON:

```
```json
{ "state": { "facts": [ "…extracted facts from the conversation…" ] …
```

**Cause (as far as I traced):** the SSE route streams model tokens for the turn, and an action's *internal* `runtime.useModel` calls (here `buildActionCompactorModelCall`) stream into the same visible channel, so intermediate model output masquerades as the reply and the action's actual final text is buried. Any action that makes internal model calls on the GUI stream path has the same exposure. Discord/connector paths are unaffected (they deliver the discrete `reply` only).

This is the same family as the narration-envelope leaks (#8162): internal artifacts crossing into user-visible output. Expected behavior: mid-action internal model calls should not stream to the client; only the turn's actual reply should.

a0:Tabe,**Describe the bug**

The resumable local-inference downloader cannot recover when a process exits after a bundle file has been fully written to its `.part` path but before the atomic rename into the bundle directory.

On restart, the sidecar SHA still matches the current manifest, so `resumableStartByte` returns the complete file size. The downloader sends `Range: bytes=<size>-`; a compliant server answers `416 Range Not Satisfiable`. The job fails while leaving the same complete partial in place, so every retry repeats the same 416. A valid multi-GB transfer is permanently stuck until the staging file is deleted manually.

**To Reproduce**

1. Put the complete expected bytes at the bundle file staging path.
2. Put the manifest SHA256 in the adjacent `.expected` sidecar.
3. Start the bundle download against an HTTP endpoint that returns 416 for a range beginning at EOF.
4. Observe the download fail before the existing SHA gate can commit the staged file.

Fails on current `develop` (`16a038447`):

<details><summary>RED test output</summary>

```text
FAIL local inference downloader stale-content robustness > commits a complete .part after restart without requesting a range past EOF
Error: HTTP 416 from model hub for elizaos/eliza-1/text/eliza-1-2b-128k.gguf
```

</details>

**Expected behavior**

Before issuing a Range request, a SHA-pinned complete partial should be recognized by its manifest SHA, atomically moved into the bundle, and have its `.expected` sidecar removed. Incomplete matching partials must continue to use normal Range resume.

Acceptance criteria:

- A complete SHA-matching `.part` is committed without another model-file request.
- The final file is byte-identical and the staging file/sidecar are gone.
- A partial strict prefix still resumes with `Range: bytes=N-`.
- Stale or unknown-provenance partials are still discarded.
- Downloader tests, plugin checks, and repository verification pass.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - downloader persistence failure has no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4/JPG: N/A - no rendered UI is involved.
- Backend logs: the exact failing consumer-boundary error is included above.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model call, prompt, action, or provider behavior is involved.
- Domain artifacts: complete `.part` plus matching `.expected` sidecar are created by the regression test.

**Additional context**

The existing final SHA256 gate prevents corrupt installation, so this is a crash-recovery/liveness bug rather than an integrity bypass. The failure window is narrow but expensive because bundle files are multi-gigabyte and the only current recovery is manual staging cleanup.a1:T789,Found while live-proving #16171 on a running Discord agent. 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)a2:Ta5d,## Summary

The `eliza-code-acp` first-use workspace provisioning path needs a deliberately designed cross-process build protocol. A proposed `O_EXCL` lock implementation was rejected during review and was **not** integrated because it still allowed concurrent/partial builds and broke paths containing spaces.

This is separate from the deterministic CI fix in #16167: mocked-native orchestrator tests now inject `ELIZA_ELIZAOS_ACP_COMMAND` and no longer enter real provisioning accidentally.

## Rejected implementation findings

- A hard-link/inode stale-reclaim check remained TOCTOU: two waiters could both validate stale lock A; waiter 1 could replace it with lock B; waiter 2 could then delete B using its already-authorized unlink.
- A wall-clock stale threshold could steal from a verified-live owner. `spawnSync()` may remain blocked after its timeout if the child traps `SIGTERM`, so age alone cannot prove ownership ended. PID reuse also makes liveness-only checks ambiguous.
- The mtime fast path could bypass a still-held lock as soon as a partial `dist/acp.js` appeared. A crash after touching/truncating the file could leave a permanently false-fresh artifact.
- The generated native command was a single unquoted string; `splitCommandLine()` breaks Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## Required design properties

- [ ] Deterministic multi-process exclusion with fencing or an equivalent proven primitive.
- [ ] No stealing from a verified-live owner based only on wall-clock age.
- [ ] Replacement-lock-safe cleanup; an old owner/waiter can never delete a successor's lock.
- [ ] Build to a temporary artifact, validate it, and publish atomically under the lock (or use an equivalent completion marker tied to the artifact).
- [ ] Crash/dead-owner recovery without PID-reuse ambiguity.
- [ ] One shared deadline across waiting and building, with bounded status/signal/stdout/stderr diagnostics.
- [ ] Structured `{ command, args }` propagation, or equivalently robust path handling, including workspaces/Bun binaries with spaces.

## Required tests

- [ ] Multiple processes reclaim one dead lock; exactly one fake build runs.
- [ ] A holder writes `partial` while keeping the lock; a waiter cannot return until the validated complete artifact is atomically published.
- [ ] A live owner beyond the nominal wait budget is never overlapped.
- [ ] Crash recovery and PID-reuse simulation.
- [ ] Workspace and Bun paths containing spaces.
- [ ] Failed build never leaves a fresh-looking executable artifact.

Do not solve this by increasing timeouts or reintroducing test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.

## Evidence plan

Attach the forced-WebSocket-loss switching proof, live create/edit Codex run, finalized trajectories, full app audit verdicts, screenshots, and MP4 walkthroughs to the PR.a6:T430,## Problem

`CliAuthSessionsService.getAndClearApiKey()` does not currently satisfy its documented exactly-once plaintext retrieval contract under concurrency.

Two poll requests can both read the same authenticated session with `consumed_at = null`, both load and decrypt the API key, and only afterward both call the unconditional `markConsumed()`. Both callers can therefore receive the plaintext credential.

This is separate from the concurrent completion/key-mint race tracked in #16240.

## Required acceptance

- Make plaintext retrieval a true single-winner operation on the primary database.
- A losing concurrent poll must receive no plaintext.
- Do not permanently consume the session if key lookup or decryption fails; define a fail-closed retry-safe transaction/state boundary.
- Preserve expiry, authentication status, and existing ownership/auth checks.
- Add a deterministic concurrent-poll integration test that proves exactly one plaintext response.

`develop` currently has no active branch ruleset or classic protection requiring PR checks. The lightweight `Develop PR` workflow and the evidence/security checks are advisory, so maintainers and agents can squash-merge before any result exists—or after a result is red.

This is no longer hypothetical. On 2026-07-13:

- #16215 opened at 02:19:20Z and merged at 02:19:55Z, 35 seconds later, before CI completed. Its evidence check subsequently failed because seven required evidence rows were absent. The body was repaired post-merge and the evidence rerun then passed.

## Risk

A PR with a type error, broken build, incomplete deterministic lane classification, secret leak, stale base, or red evidence can be merged before GitHub reports it. Post-merge CI detects damage only after `develop` and downstream deploy triggers have advanced.

## Proposed safe shape

1. Add one stable hosted-runner aggregate check for the current lightweight PR gate. It must run with `if: always()` and fail unless the required component jobs reached allowed terminal states.
2. Include at minimum:
   - lint
   - typecheck
   - build
   - deterministic lane coverage
   - stale-base guard
   - PR title/evidence
   - security/gitleaks
   - changed-file coverage when applicable
3. Install an active `develop` ruleset requiring that stable aggregate, PR review flow, deletion protection, and non-fast-forward protection.
4. Do not restore the removed heavy merge queue or make scheduled/exhaustive/device/aesthetic lanes required.
5. Give emergency bypass only to explicitly named maintainers, audit every bypass, and never grant an automation-wide silent bypass.
6. Add a low-risk green canary and an intentionally red canary under active monitoring to prove enforcement before closing.

## Acceptance

- A PR cannot merge while the aggregate is pending, missing, cancelled, timed out, or failed.
- A path-skipped component is classified explicitly rather than making the aggregate disappear.
- A normal focused PR reaches a mergeable state without waiting for heavy post-merge suites.
- A red canary is visibly blocked; a green canary merges after the required aggregate succeeds.
- Ruleset JSON, exact canary PRs/runs, and rollback instructions are attached.
- #13386 is closed as obsolete or redirected here; #14051 is updated to distinguish throughput work from enforcement.

- #16202: merged before standard PR validation; post-merge push is the first full proof.

- Runtime proof: full `packages/core` suite and focused integration results will be attached to the PR.b0:T975,## Problem

`RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT` can report a different Claude/Codex model than `plugin-cli-inference` actually invokes.

PR #16310 added static `displayModel` metadata by reading `process.env` when the plugin module is constructed. The handlers, however, resolve model names from `runtime.getSetting()` for each agent and tier:

- Claude large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_MODEL`
- Claude planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting
- Codex large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_MODEL`
- Codex planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting

Core deliberately gives per-agent runtime settings precedence and does not read `process.env` from `getSetting()`. In a multi-agent process, or whenever a character setting differs from the host environment, the handler calls one model while its context provider claims another. The new unit tests only exercise environment-derived metadata and therefore do not cover the real runtime boundary.

## Required fix

- Make model-registration display metadata able to resolve an ordered list of runtime setting keys before a static provider default.
- Declare CLI-inference metadata per tier:
  - large tiers: the backend's large-model runtime key, then its canonical default;
  - planner: planner-model runtime key, then large-model runtime key, then the canonical default.
- Preserve serializable, handler-free registration metadata.
- Add integration coverage that constructs a real `AgentRuntime` with runtime values intentionally conflicting with `process.env`, registers the plugin models, invokes `RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT`, and proves the reported model matches the handler's effective model for both a large slot and the planner fallback chain.
- Keep inert/native-planner behavior unchanged.
- Replace change-narration comments with durable system rationale per the repository comment policy.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Per-agent/character settings override host environment values in model self-reporting.
- [ ] Planner metadata follows the same planner -> large -> default precedence as the handler.
- [ ] Claude and Codex backends are covered.
- [ ] Existing core and plugin-cli-inference tests pass.
- [ ] `bun run verify` passes on a branch rebased onto current `develop`.
- [ ] Exact-head evidence includes the provider output and handler-selected model from a real runtime, manually reviewed and attached inline to the PR.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one process-level verifier for installed elizaOS launchers and uses it in all five Snap, Debian, and Flatpak smoke steps. The verifier runs the actual installed command for both `--version` and `--help`, requires a zero exit, requires the exact release version, and requires a Commander `Usage:` section.

Contract tests exercise real child executables and parse the workflow YAML to prevent masked launcher checks from returning. Flatpak AppStream metadata now points at existing reviewed application screenshots rather than deleted documentation assets.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking release-pipeline hardening).

# Documentation changes needed?

The Flatpak submission README now documents its repository-hosted screenshot source of truth.

Bug fixes and reliability improvements.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes release tooling and the manifest preparation order used by the release workflow. Failure modes are intentionally stricter: malformed workspace metadata, ambiguous registry responses, changed manifests, conflicting integrity, moved Git refs, or inconsistent retry evidence now halt the release. The new unit and real-process integration suites cover successful publication, failure before mutation, partial publication, exact-integrity resume, registry restart, atomic Git rejection, ref movement, and idempotent retries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This adds the fail-closed, immutable release primitives requested by #16276:

- A canonical, deterministic release contract records the source/expected commit, exact version and channel, explicit package cohort and directories, dependency graph and order, manifest identity, entrypoint metadata, and the real packed tarball path, size, SHA-512 digest, SRI integrity, and npm shasum. `2.0.3-beta.8`, `.9`, and `.10` remain permanently reserved.
- One shared workspace resolver now serves release scripts and app-core discovery. Invalid manifests, non-file manifests, duplicate package names, and unexpected filesystem failures surface instead of disappearing.
- Candidate creation requires a clean exact Git HEAD, executes one caller-supplied build exactly once, verifies manifests did not change, invokes `npm pack --json --ignore-scripts` once per package, validates the packed package identity and entrypoints, and records immutable real `.tgz` artifacts. Candidate state uses canonical JSON, ordered phase transitions, atomic replacement, and an exclusive writer lock.
- Registry inspection treats only HTTP 404 as missing. Authentication, authorization, throttling, server, transport, redirect, and malformed-response failures are distinct hard errors. Publication preflights the complete cohort before mutation, skips only exact-integrity matches, resumes missing packages from the recorded tarballs, promotes the requested channel only after full verification, and binds retries to the recorded registry.
- Git publication pushes only the explicitly named branch and tag in one atomic operation with an exact old-branch lease. Remote movement, non-fast-forward updates, conflicting tags, retry evidence changes, and reserved tags fail without mutation; matching annotated or lightweight tags dereference to the expected commit and no-op.
- `replace-workspace-versions.js`, `restore-workspace-refs.js`, and `release-set-public-access.mjs` now delegate to shared transactional manifest primitives. Rewrites use exact target versions, preserve byte-exact originals in an integrity journal, roll back on write/journal failure, and refuse restoration over a changed manifest.
- The only release workflow behavior change is the required access-before-reference-journal ordering, so the journal snapshot and packed manifests describe the same final bytes. The broader `release.yaml` orchestration remains outside this primitive-focused issue.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and non-breaking release-infrastructure feature.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was required and is included in `.github/workflows/README.md`, covering the immutable artifact/state contract, exact resume rules, reserved versions, registry/Git safety properties, commands, and the deliberate orchestration boundary.

## Where should a reviewer start?

3. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-contract.test.ts` for cohort, dependency graph, exact-version, reserved-version, deterministic serialization, and state-machine invariants.
4. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-candidate.test.ts` and `release-manifests.test.ts` for immutable `.tgz` creation and transactional manifest behavior.

```bash

- Focused suite: **55 pass, 0 fail, 163 assertions** across seven files; overall focused line coverage **91.26%**.
- Real Verdaccio: publication was interrupted by terminating the registry before package two, leaving one exact-integrity match and one missing version; restarting the same registry/storage resumed only the missing tarball, verified both packages, promoted `beta`, removed the staging tag, made the next run a no-op, and rejected a changed registry URL.
- Real bare Git: an update hook rejected the tag and the atomic push changed neither branch nor tag; retry after removing the hook moved exactly the named branch and tag while leaving an unrelated local tag unpublished. Moved branch state, conflicting tags, changed retry evidence, and reserved tags all failed without mutation.
- Full verification: **573 successful / 573 total** Turbo tasks; build/typecheck model, dependency graph, secret-leak, script, and test-realness audits passed; **28/28** dist-path consumers typechecked.
- No public npm package, dist-tag, Git branch, or Git tag was created or modified.

b4:Tddf,## Stack

This is a reviewable hardening commit stacked on #16192 at exact base `47b5f2054145b8d4102e7471b8f26645c7802574`. It targets `fix/voice-realtime-ui-staging-wiring` so it can be reviewed and merged into the parent branch without overwriting Solace's work.

## What this closes

- obtains a fresh one-use consent nonce for every initial mint and reconnect;
- cancels provisional microphone and playback setup, including stalled AudioWorklet loading, without leaking a hot mic or AudioContext;
- serializes reconnects and prevents stale sockets, stops, and rapid identity changes from resurrecting or clobbering a newer lifecycle;
- makes actual composer clicks cancel pending realtime starts while preserving speaking-time barge-in;
- keeps manual and continuous voice on one mic owner, with batch fallback unchanged when realtime is unavailable;
- surfaces autoplay unlock state and preserves low-latency streaming PCM playback;
- correlates mint/turn telemetry without exposing tokens, prompts, transcripts, or raw identifiers in the always-on HUD;
- fails staging deploys closed when Deepgram, Cartesia, or bridge auth is absent;
- keeps production realtime explicitly off, blocks repository-key fallback there, and makes a future enable require dedicated auth plus complete provider/runtime configuration;
- preserves the parent branch's Worker `ArrayBuffer` transport fix and browser/UI evidence.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds a consolidated `AccountManagementPanel` under Models & Providers so account-backed providers are managed in one place.
- Converts `AddAccountDialog` from provider-only entry to a consolidated picker when no provider is preselected.
- Separates chat API-key providers from coding-subscription providers with explicit eligibility labels.
- Removes provider-scoped account lists from advanced API-key provider panels so settings reads as Intelligence, Model Configuration, Accounts, Voice, Advanced.
- Keeps subscription activation available from the consolidated Accounts group, including local-only recovery.
- Restores pending OAuth/device sessions by reopening the add-account dialog for any pending provider.
- Adds a unit test for provider picker eligibility so Claude subscription remains coding-agent-only, not default chat.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement, non-breaking UI/UX change.

# Outcome

Replaces the legacy single-job release script with an explicit three-stage transaction:

1. A credential-free candidate job verifies the exact repository/ref/SHA, builds and packs the reviewed cohort once, and records immutable plan, cohort, manifest, entrypoint, and tarball integrity.
2. A least-privilege npm job authenticates the planned publisher, binds the normalized registry before mutation, stages missing immutable versions under a cohort-derived candidate tag, verifies the full cohort, promotes the public channel, and removes the staging tags.
3. A finalizer re-reads npm without credentials, binds the Git push destination, pushes only the exact planned annotated tag, and creates or verifies a GitHub Release whose repository, tag, target commit, and prerelease identity match the plan.

Every mutation command requires the complete release identity plus the canonical plan digest. Exclusive state locks and monotonic `registry-bound` / `git-bound` transitions make interruption retries idempotent without permitting a target, publisher, candidate-tag, ref, or artifact swap.

# Pack-once invariant

The obsolete `verify-npm-pack-dist.mjs` helper is removed because its only caller was the legacy workflow and it rebuilt plus dry-packed packages a second time. Validation is not reduced: candidate construction now performs the single real `npm pack`, checks the actual `.tgz` bytes, verifies every declared `main` / `module` / `types` / `bin` / concrete `exports` target is present, records SHA-512 hex and SRI, and revalidates the same immutable tarballs before every side effect. Removing the legacy helper is what preserves the issue's build/test/pack-once contract and clears the script-orphan audit.

# Risk and rollout

Risk is high because this is public release infrastructure. The workflow remains manual/reusable, uses one global non-cancelling concurrency group, reserves failed beta versions, separates trusted tooling from candidate source, and never pushes a branch. This PR did not contact public npm for publication or create/update any public Git tag or GitHub Release.

b8:T1a40,"## Problem\n\nNothing deterministic stood between a sub-agent's \"done\" claim and the task being declared done:\n\n- **No residuals check existed anywhere** — no `git status --porcelain`, no unpushed-commit check; \"tests pass / no leftovers\" was decided by an LLM reading self-reported envelopes and regex-mined transcript text.\n- `validateTask` wrote `status:\"done\"` **directly**, bypassing the lifecycle transition table; `humanOverride` could promote a task from ANY status with a fabricated default evidence string, via plain `POST /tasks/:id/validate`.\n- Operator paths (`retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent`/`restartTask`/`reopenTask`) also bypassed the table, contradicting the table module's \"single legal way\" contract.\n- The user-facing completion relay never consulted verification state — the user could be told \"done\" while verification was still running or failing.\n- Criteria-free tasks skipped verification entirely; a dead duplicate of the goal-contract module diverged from the live one.\n\n## Fix\n\n- New `completion-residuals.ts` — deterministic gate run **before any model spend** in `autoVerifyCompletion` and before honoring `passed:true` in `validateTask`: real `git status --porcelain`, `git rev-list @{u}..HEAD` (when an upstream exists), envelope legs (`testResults[].exitCode !== 0`, non-empty `residualRisks`). Statuses: `clean | residuals | unverifiable`. Fail-closed (`unverifiable`) when the task **expects a repo** (`session.repo` or `task.boundRepo`); for unbound tasks the git legs run opportunistically when the workdir is a real git worktree and skip otherwise (every ACP session has a scratch workdir — a voice/Q&A task must not be blocked by a non-git scratch dir; caught by merged-tree scenario verification). The snapshot is persisted on task metadata + validation events so the UI shows what blocked (or was overridden at) completion.\n- **All durable status writes now route through the transition table**: new `human_override_passed/failed` triggers (non-terminal states only), `restarted` made total; `retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent` via `retrying`, `restartTask`/`reopenTask` via `restarted`/`reopened`.
